Round-2 review of the REVISED codex design for issue #4525 (positron-ai/tron)

Did the revised design for typed KV-cache tensors close the round-1 findings, and is it implementable now?

Reviewed 2026-09-22 UTC by Claude (Fable 5.1) for jhan. Design reviewed: status/design-new-tensor-type.html, revised by codex after the round-1 review (revision finalized 2026-09-22 03:06 UTC, 83,567 bytes). Sources pinned to main f46e48ba (the design's snapshot), main 98bb8cb2 (the tip the design records), the live main tip 2880c3aa9b (fetched 2026-09-22 ~04:00 UTC, unchanged since 01:14 UTC) and PR 4424 head 30c4ac82cb.
Short version

The revision resolved 35 of the 58 round-1 findings, left 20 partly resolved, and deferred the 3 issue-body edits to jhan; nothing regressed.

Four new major gaps remain: the 8-lane build guidance orders code and test runs for a configuration whose baseline tests do not compile, the NaN test rule ignores that TRON_IGNORE_NAN is on in every production build, the child section never switches the K storage member to the new owner, and the definition of done depends on reviewer answers that have no place to be recorded.

Verdict: accept with changes. After those four edits and an honest status column in section 9, another agent can implement the parent from this page.

New findings

Verdict

1. Words used here

tron
the inference program under test; its source is the positron-ai/tron repository
PR 4424, the child
the open draft pull request "VNNI K: store the K cache of 128-dimension heads in the AMX VNNI layout, with a shared block save" (branch jhan-amx-vnniK, head 30c4ac82cb). After the new parent PR exists, PR 4424 is rebased onto it and is then called the child
the parent PR
the new pull request that issue #4525 asks for: typed access to the existing V and K caches on main, with no new layout
Ben, the reviewer
the tron maintainer who reviewed PR 4424 on 2026-09-21 (a worker-index question, a review asking for typed tensors, and an API sketch). The revised design calls him "the reviewer"
codex, the design, the revision
codex is the agent jhan asked to answer Ben; the design is the page it wrote, status/design-new-tensor-type.html; the revision is the version of that page finalized 2026-09-22 03:06 UTC after the round-1 review
round 1, F.. and C.. ids
the first Claude review of the original design (2026-09-22 ~03:00 UTC), archived as claude-review-design-new-tensor-type-r1.html. Its 58 retained findings are F01-F59 (finders) and C01-C09 (completeness critic); ten candidates F06, F07, F09, F18, F22, F33, F36, F44, F53, F55 were rejected. The revised design cites these ids in brackets, for example [F05]
N.. and M.. ids
new findings of this round: N.. from the eight finders, M.. from the completeness critic
issue #4525
the GitHub issue codex created for the parent PR; jhan edited its title and first sentence on 2026-09-22 00:10 UTC; unchanged since
KV cache, K, V
the saved key (K) and value (V) vectors of earlier tokens; attention reads them
page, slot, head, D
a page holds 64 token positions; a slot is the cache region of one layer (or of several layers that share it); a head is one attention head; D is the head size, the number of values per token per head (128 for PR 4424's models)
bf16
the 16-bit brain floating-point format used for cached K and V
AMX, AVX-512, 16-lane and 8-lane builds
AMX and AVX-512 are CPU instruction sets. tron's vector width TRON_CHUNK_SIZE is 16 fp32 values with AVX-512 (the 16-lane build) and 8 without (the 8-lane build); the 8-lane build stores V row-major, so it has no pair-interleaved V
packed V (pair-interleaved V)
today's V storage in the 16-lane build: for each dimension, tokens 2p and 2p+1 sit next to each other. Element offset = (token/2)*(2*D) + 2*dim + token%2
native K
today's K storage: one token's D values consecutive in memory (row-major)
blocked packed K (VNNI K)
PR 4424's K storage for 128-dimension heads: two consecutive dimensions of one token share a 4-byte unit, and 16 tokens share one 64-byte line
expr, view, const_view, tensor
tron's expression interface (h/tron/kernels/expr.hpp), its typed pointer views over regularly laid-out memory and their read-only form (h/tron/tensor/view.hpp), and its owning arrays (h/tron/tensor/tensor.hpp)
v_vnni_tensor, v_vnni_view, v_vnni_row
the revised design's names for the packed-V owner (the aligned array inside the cache block), the typed rank-2 view over it, and its logical row expression
append_v_row, load_row, copy_token, copy_plane
the revised design's typed bulk operations: write one token's V row into the packed plane (even token zeroes its odd partner), read one row out, copy one token between planes, copy a whole plane
kv_block
the struct that holds K then V for one head of one slot in one page (h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp)
set_v, get_v, append_v, scaled_v, v_data
today's V functions in kv_cache.hpp: write one token, read one token, copy one token between pages, compute the weighted V sum, and return the raw pointer of a V plane
save_v_impl
the model function (h/tron/models/model.hpp) that stores one token's V into the cache during a forward pass; it calls page::set_v
Zero-Initialized V Slots invariant
the rule in kv_cache.hpp that every even-token V write zeroes its odd partner row. The weighted sum reads a whole pair even when the token count is odd, and NaN times 0 is NaN, so the padding row must be zero
arena, book
an arena is one allocated memory region holding cache blocks; book is the class that owns the arenas and the pages
EAGLE
the speculative model; its cache adds one extra physical KV slot and a separate embedding buffer (x_data); it requires uniform geometry
GOF, page_info
group of four: four tokens of K and V staged for the FPGA's hardware KV cache; the staging code reaches a page's rows through the page_info callbacks in h/tron/gof.hpp (namespace tron::hardware)
CI, Nix test build, TRON_AMX_DISPATCH, TRON_K_VNNI
CI is the automated build and test. The Nix test build is the required CI lane. The two CMake options compile the AMX attention kernels and PR 4424's K layout; the Nix lane sets neither. Issue #3997 tracks the AMX gap
checker, re-grader, finder, verifier, judge
review agents of this round. A checker graded a batch of round-1 findings against the revision; two re-graders (a source-truth lens and a fairness lens) independently re-graded each; the majority of the three decided. Eight finders looked for new defects; three verifiers tried to refute each; a finding survived when at least two could not. Three judges gave independent implementability verdicts
resolved, partly, deferred, unresolved, regressed
disposition statuses; the table in section 3 gives the meaning of each
blocker, major, minor, note
severities; the table in section 4 gives the meaning of each

2. What changed between the two versions of the design

The revision keeps the structure of the original design and adds the parts the round-1 review asked for. Codex changed no production code, posted nothing to GitHub, and edited no issue; it says so in the page header and in section 10.

ItemOriginal design (reviewed in round 1)Revised design (reviewed here)
Size and sections67,216 bytes; 10 sections83,567 bytes; 10 sections; section 9 is now a disposition table of the 58 round-1 findings and section 2 has a "Questions for the reviewer" block
Non-ASCII characters26759 (em dash in the title, curly quotes, arrows, multiplication signs)
Type namesvnni_tensor / vnni_view / vnni_row for V; k_vnni_* for the childv_vnni_tensor / v_vnni_view / v_vnni_row; native_k_view and const_native_k_view aliases for K; k_vnni_* stays in the child
Scope of the parentmatrix and row expressions, slices, iterators, generic row.set / matrix.set / set_pair, owner expression constructorexpression reads and typed bulk operations only; slices, iteration, generic assignment and the owner expression constructor are listed as "Explicitly deferred"
Model V save pathnot namedsave_v_impl -> page::set_v -> append_v_row named in the accessor table and in step 3; a "One cache row-write rule" table gives the even/odd partner behavior
Accessor mappingabsenta table with one row per existing accessor (page::k, set_v, get_v, v/v_ptr, v_data/v_base, append_v, scaled_v, kernels)
Revision table98bb8cb2 labelled a stale local copy98bb8cb2 labelled the main tip recorded when the design was written, 79 commits after the older snapshot; the older snapshot labelled "the older PR base, not the main tip"
Arena construction"placement construction"; std::start_lifetime_as offered as an optionthe exact array-new form with a runtime bound; construct_at / T() / T{} forbidden; start_lifetime_as removed; uniform and heterogeneous enumeration rules; EAGLE slot and x_data distinguished
8-lane buildthree configurations listed without flagsboth chunk widths must compile the packed types; exact CMake flags per configuration; the 8-lane lane has no CI coverage, stated
CI paragraph"A required CI job must build with AMX enabled"the AMX gap is separately tracked by issue #3997; the Nix flag change is a decision for jhan and the CI owners
Ben's sketch"Your interpretation is mostly right"; "No clarification from Ben is needed""The interpretation of the reviewer's reservation is right"; four questions with proposed answers, marked as proposals pending reviewer agreement
Issue #4525 and the PR threadno reply plannedstill no reply or issue edit; the design says a future PR reply should link the issue and the questions, and that the issue-body additions are recorded in the design only

The revised page cites round-1 findings by id in brackets, for example [F05], and links each id to this page's anchors. This round keeps an anchor for every round-1 id (section 3), so those links still resolve. The round-1 page itself is archived unchanged as claude-review-design-new-tensor-type-r1.html, with its votes on claude-review-design-new-tensor-type-r1-votes.html.

The live main tip did not move during this round: 2880c3aa9b (2026-09-22 01:14 UTC). Among the design's step-list files, only t/CMakeLists.txt changed (2 lines) between the tip the design records (98bb8cb2) and the live tip. Issue #4525 and PR 4424 are unchanged since round 1: no reply after Ben's sketch, no issue comment, no cross-reference.

3. Did the revision address the 58 round-1 findings?

Each of the 58 round-1 findings was checked against the revised text by one checker agent and then independently re-graded by two adversarial re-graders (a source-truth lens and a fairness lens). The status below is the majority of the three. No id had a split vote (three different statuses). Three ids were not unanimous (F12 and F38 majority partly, F43 majority resolved); I accepted the majority in each case and changed no status myself.

A finding graded partly is not a failure of the revision. In most of the 20 cases the headline recommendation is met and what is missing is a refinement the round-1 verifiers had recorded under the finding. Section 4 turns the residuals that matter into new findings (N.. ids) so they have evidence and a recommendation of their own; the table below names them.

Resolved 35Partly 203
Resolved = the revised text meets the round-1 recommendation in substance, and every checkable fact in the fix is right · Partly = the substance is there, but a concrete part of the recommendation is missing or a small slip remains · Deferred = the design explicitly leaves it to separate work; the remark says whether that is acceptable
StatusMeaningCount

4. New findings on the revised design

Eight finders read the revised text through one lens each (C++ interface, behavior and arena construction, process and CI, handoff actionability, fidelity to Ben, simplicity and scope, formulas and page quality, the child's needs). Their candidates, plus defects the disposition checkers found in the fixes themselves, were merged and each put to three verifiers who tried to refute it; a finding survived only if at least two could not. A completeness critic then added candidates that went through the same verification. Severities are the lead's final grades; where they differ from the finder's grade, the finding says so under Lead's reading.

Findings in full

Major N01. Both-chunk-width rule forces unused 8-lane packed bodies

Where
5. Interface design, Build and include boundaries; 8. Verification, Build configurations
Round-1 link
touches F11
Claim
Design decision I disagree with, plus an ambiguity: the rule to compile the packed types under both chunk widths and to derive the pair-chunk width from chunk_size makes the implementing agent write AVX2 (8-lane) bodies for append_v_row, load_row, copy_token, chunk and bf16_chunk, and run the packed-V test rows in the 8-lane configuration, for a layout the 8-lane cache never uses. Only class completeness is required there: the fake kernel takes the view by value, and completing a class template instantiates the class body, not its member function bodies. AMX dispatch is a compile error in the 8-lane build, so no 8-lane code calls any packed operation. The test rows in section 8 are not chunk-guarded, so t_llama_unit would instantiate the bodies at 8 lanes unless the agent guesses to guard them.
Design says
Compile all three packed V types under both TRON_CHUNK_SIZE == 8 and == 16. Derive a pair chunk's width from chunk_size. Keep row-major V in the 8-lane cache; the packed type still must be complete for the unconditionally compiled AMX test substitutes.
Evidence
h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:30-35@2880c3aa9b (#if defined(TRON_AMX_DISPATCH) && TRON_CHUNK_SIZE != 16 ... #error); t/t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp:166-190@2880c3aa9b (fake definitions outside any #ifdef, weights_times_v_128x4(const bf16*, const float*, size_t, float* output) ignores the plane argument); t/CMakeLists.txt:596@2880c3aa9b (unconditional registration); kv_cache.hpp:2195-2202@2880c3aa9b (8-lane v is a row-major array) and design line 322 ("Keep native K and 8-lane V as their existing arrays"); model.hpp:2833-2837@2880c3aa9b (8-lane save uses pg.v<>().set(slice(...))); r1-findings F11 verifier correction: "completing a class template for a by-value parameter instantiates the class body but not its member function bodies". Design lines 270, 395-399 (test rows without a chunk guard), 413 ("Include unconditional fake AMX definitions").
Correction from verification
Ambiguity plus a design-cost objection (not a mandate): design.txt:270 ("Compile all three packed V types under both TRON_CHUNK_SIZE == 8 and == 16. Derive a pair chunk's width from chunk_size.") and design.txt:220 (chunk(i) contract in chunk_size terms) conflict with design.txt:352 ("Keep the existing vector bodies here"), because the existing bodies are 16-lane-only _mm512 code under #if TRON_CHUNK_SIZE == 16 (kv_cache.hpp:2281-2320). The design never says whether append_v_row, load_row, copy_token, chunk and bf16_chunk must work at 8 lanes or may stay 16-lane-only, so the implementing agent must guess; the width-generic guess yields untested AVX2 packed bodies that no 8-lane code calls (AMX call sites are inside #ifdef TRON_AMX_DISPATCH, self_attention.hpp:1571-1701, and that macro is a #error at 8 lanes, :30-35; 8-lane V stays row-major, kv_cache.hpp:2199-2201, design.txt:322). Only class completeness is needed at 8 lanes, for the by-value fake definitions in t/t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp:166-190 (registered unconditionally, t/CMakeLists.txt:596). Recommended text: keep the class definitions complete in both builds; compile the operation bodies and every packed-V test case (design.txt:396, 398, the rows that build a packed owner directly) only under TRON_CHUNK_SIZE == 16; keep typed kernel declarations and fakes unconditional. Do NOT mark the 8-lane configuration compile-only: the parent changes 8-lane code paths (typed set_v wrapper design.txt:254, v_tensor accessor and v_data/v_base removal :255, typed scaled_v_expr constructor :257), so the existing row-major t_llama_unit cases must run there; note that page::set_v exists today only under TRON_CHUNK_SIZE == 16 (kv_cache.hpp:1616-1674) while t_llama_unit.cpp:1723, 2690, 2708 call it unguarded, so the 8-lane baseline of t_llama_unit must be established first, as design.txt:413 already says.
Ambiguity plus omission, not a forced wrong choice. (1) The revised design gives conflicting cues about the 8-lane (TRON_CHUNK_SIZE == 8, AVX2) build. Line 270 says "Compile all three packed V types under both TRON_CHUNK_SIZE == 8 and == 16. Derive a pair chunk's width from chunk_size", which reads as width-generic bodies. Line 225 specifies extraction mechanics only "For the 16-lane build", and step 1 (line 352) says "Keep the existing vector bodies here"; those bodies are the AVX-512 _mm512_* code at kv_cache.hpp:2000-2007 and 2300-2320@2880c3aa9b. The design never says whether the operation bodies (append_v_row, load_row, copy_token, chunk, bf16_chunk) must work at 8 lanes or may stay 16-lane-only. It does not force AVX2 bodies: member and function template bodies instantiate only on use (r1 F11 verifier correction), the fakes only need a complete class (t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp:180-188@2880c3aa9b ignore their arguments), and the 8-lane cache never calls a packed operation (kv_cache.hpp:2195-2202, model.hpp:2834-2836@2880c3aa9b). Add one sentence: the class definitions are complete in both builds; the operation bodies stay 16-lane code and may sit under #if TRON_CHUNK_SIZE == 16, matching the existing guards at kv_cache.hpp:1616, 1866, 2281@2880c3aa9b; no AVX2 pair extraction is required. (2) The finding's sentence "run the packed-V test rows in the 8-lane configuration" is not in the design: the 8-lane row (line 413) asks only to "Establish the unmodified-baseline result" and "Include unconditional fake AMX definitions". The finding also misses that t_llama_unit at live main cannot compile at 8 lanes (inferred from source, not built): page::set_v and page::get_v are declared and defined only under #if TRON_CHUNK_SIZE == 16 (kv_cache.hpp:1616-1674 declarations, 2281-2532 definitions@2880c3aa9b), and t_llama_unit.cpp calls them without a guard in a plain TEST_CASE ("book.append()", lines 2483-2489 concrete book_t; set_v/get_v at 2533-2540) and in the "scaled_v" TEMPLATE_TEST_CASE (page_t* p at 1710; p->set_v at 1723). So the 8-lane row can only be a compile check of libtron plus the fake-kernel TU; new behavior cases cannot instantiate packed bodies at 8 lanes in t_llama_unit because that file already fails there. Rewrite the 8-lane row as: "Compile-only for the parent (libtron and t_amx_dispatch_dtype). t_llama_unit is expected not to build at 8 lanes on unmodified main because it calls set_v/get_v outside a chunk guard; confirm with a cmake --preset native -DAVX512=OFF build of main before attributing anything to the parent." Keep "Include unconditional fake AMX definitions."
Ambiguity (c), minor: design line 270 ("Compile all three packed V types under both TRON_CHUNK_SIZE == 8 and == 16. Derive a pair chunk's width from chunk_size.") adopts round-1 F11's option A literally, but the round-1 verifier correction already established that only class completeness is needed at 8 lanes (the fake at t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp:185 takes the view by value; member bodies instantiate only on use). The design should add: "In the 8-lane build the three packed V classes need only be complete so the unconditional typed declarations and fake definitions compile. The packed operation bodies (append_v_row, load_row, copy_token, chunk, bf16_chunk) are the moved 16-lane code; no 8-lane caller or test instantiates them, and no AVX2 pair-extraction implementation is required." Drop N01's claims that the design forces AVX2 bodies and that it runs packed-V test rows at 8 lanes: the operations are templates that instantiate only on use, page::set_v at 8 lanes forwards to row .set() (line 254), and unmodified main's t_llama_unit does not compile at chunk 8 (non-template TEST_CASE "scaled_v padding NaN" at t/t_llama_unit.cpp:2658-2761@2880c3aa9b calls set_v/get_v, which exist only under TRON_CHUNK_SIZE == 16 at kv_cache.hpp:1616-1725), which line 413's baseline instruction already covers. Keep N01's optional sentence for section 8 marking the 8-lane row as compile-only for packed V.
Recommendation
Replace line 270 with: "The class definitions of v_vnni_tensor, v_vnni_view and v_vnni_row are complete in both builds (their members are a bf16 array, a pointer, and small integers, no vector types). Their operation bodies (append_v_row, load_row, copy_token, chunk, bf16_chunk) and every packed-V test case compile only under TRON_CHUNK_SIZE == 16; the 8-lane build implements no pair extraction. The typed kernel declarations and the fake definitions stay unconditional." In section 8, mark the 8-lane row as a compile-only check for the parent (no packed behavior case runs there).
Lead's reading
Two of the three verifiers graded this minor on its own: the class-completeness point was already in round-1 F11's verifier correction, and the design's sentence adopts F11's option A literally. I keep major because of what M01 and N61 add: the same 8-lane row tells the agent to run t_llama_unit and t_heterogeneous_scheduler in a configuration where, at unmodified main, neither compiles (page::set_v and page::get_v exist only under TRON_CHUNK_SIZE == 16). Taken together, the 8-lane guidance would send the implementer into a baseline failure and into writing AVX2 pair-extraction bodies that nothing calls. One rewrite of the both-chunk-width sentence and of the 8-lane row fixes all three findings.
Found by
simplicity-scope, handoff-actionability, cpp-interface; 3 of 3 verifiers could not refute it (votes and corrections)

Major N02. TRON_IGNORE_NAN on in every non-Debug build; NaN row build-dependent

Where
6. Behavior the implementation must preserve (Conversions and weighted sums) and 8. Verification (Bits and conversions row)
Round-1 link
touches F41
Claim
Omission: the design says the generic conversion skips its NaN correction when TRON_IGNORE_NAN (the compile-time switch that disables NaN handling in the SIMD conversion helpers) is defined, but it does not say when that happens. TRON_IGNORE_NAN is defined for every non-Debug configuration, as a PUBLIC compile option of the tron library, so every test that links tron inherits it. All three configurations in the design's section 8 table use cmake --preset native, and the native preset is RelWithDebInfo. The required Nix CI build is also RelWithDebInfo. In every one of those builds fp32s_to_bf16s (the generic bf16 conversion) truncates exactly like the packed set_v path, so 0x7F800001 becomes 0x7F80 (+Inf) on both paths. Only the Debug presets asan, tsan and coverage produce the 0x7F81 result the design's paragraph describes. The section 8 row 'Keep packed append and generic expression conversion expectations separate' therefore cannot be implemented with fixed NaN expectations: a value chosen from the design's prose passes in Debug and fails under --preset native and in required CI, or the reverse. The same build dependence applies to fp16 NaN sources, where the two paths give 0x7FC0 versus 0x7FC1 in Debug and identical 0x7FC0 elsewhere. The round-1 F41 verifier correction stated this dependence explicitly; the revised text carries only the conditional clause.
Design says
It skips that correction when TRON_IGNORE_NAN is defined. This preserves a NaN but does not necessarily make it a quiet NaN. Neither path rounds to nearest. Preserve each existing path separately. [S5] [S7]
Evidence
src/tron/CMakeLists.txt:292-295@2880c3aa9b: target_compile_options(tron PUBLIC "$<$<AND:${CXX_GccOrClang},$<NOT:$<CONFIG:Debug>>>:-O3;-DTRON_IGNORE_NAN;-DTRON_IGNORE_FTZ>" ...) (same at 314-318 for tron_model_compile_config). CMakePresets.json@2880c3aa9b: native inherits base-ninja with CMAKE_BUILD_TYPE RelWithDebInfo; deb RelWithDebInfo; asan/tsan/coverage Debug. nix/cmake-tron-test-build.nix:80@2880c3aa9b: -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=RelWithDebInfo. t/CMakeLists.txt:586-592@2880c3aa9b: add_catch_test(NAME t_llama_unit LIBRARIES tron pos_fake ...). h/tron/simd/bf16.hpp:93-108@2880c3aa9b: #ifdef TRON_IGNORE_NAN uint32x16 y = cast_uint32(x); #else ... _mm512_mask_or_epi32(xi, mask, xi, _mm512_set1_epi32(0x00010000)); #endif y = _mm512_srli_epi32(y, 16);. kv_cache.hpp:2342, 2382@2880c3aa9b: _mm512_srli_epi32(_mm512_castps_si512(lo_fp32), 16). design.txt:318 (the paragraph), 396 (test row), 409-413 (build table with cmake --preset native); design.txt has no occurrence of 'Debug' or 'RelWithDebInfo'. r1-findings.md F41 verifier correction 3: 'src/tron/CMakeLists.txt:267-270 defines TRON_IGNORE_NAN for every non-Debug build, and the CI Nix test build is RelWithDebInfo'.
Correction from verification
Severity minor, not major. The omission is narrower than "the design does not say when that happens" alone suggests: design.txt:318 already carries the conditional clause naming TRON_IGNORE_NAN, so the missing pieces are (1) one sentence that the macro is defined for every non-Debug configuration [src/tron/CMakeLists.txt:293 and :316 @2880c3aa9b, PUBLIC on tron], that the native and deb presets [CMakePresets.json:37, :74] and the Nix test build [nix/cmake-tron-test-build.nix:80] are RelWithDebInfo, and that the 0x7F81 result appears only in the asan, tsan and coverage presets [CMakePresets.json:89, :102, :114]; and (2) in the section 8 "Bits and conversions" row (design.txt:396), a note that generic-path NaN expectations depend on TRON_IGNORE_NAN and must be guarded or avoided. Also the section 9 F41 row (design.txt:474, "Accepted; example corrected") should record that the round-1 second verifier correction (build-type dependence) was not applied, or apply it.
Recommendation
Add after the TRON_IGNORE_NAN sentence: 'TRON_IGNORE_NAN is defined for every non-Debug configuration (src/tron/CMakeLists.txt:292-295, a PUBLIC option of tron). The native and deb presets and the required Nix test build are RelWithDebInfo, so in every configuration in section 8 both paths truncate identically (0x7F800001 -> 0x7F80 on both). The 0x7F81 result appears only in the Debug presets asan, tsan and coverage.' In the section 8 'Bits and conversions' row add: 'NaN expectations for the generic path depend on TRON_IGNORE_NAN; either guard them with #ifdef TRON_IGNORE_NAN or check the generic path only with finite, denormal and infinity inputs, which convert identically in both paths.'
Lead's reading
I verified the macro myself: src/tron/CMakeLists.txt at 2880c3aa9b defines -DTRON_IGNORE_NAN for every non-Debug configuration as a PUBLIC option, and the native, deb and Nix test builds are RelWithDebInfo. So the design's sentence about the generic conversion setting a mantissa bit describes Debug builds only; in every build the design lists, both paths truncate identically. The consequence is a test-expectation trap, which is why this stays major although one verifier said minor: a NaN expectation written from the design's prose passes under asan and fails under the required Nix lane, or the reverse. The fp16 example round 1 gave was wrong for a second reason, which codex spotted: the hardware conversion quiets the NaN, so no fp16 NaN becomes infinity on either path.
Found by
behavior-arena, formulas-and-page; 3 of 3 verifiers could not refute it (votes and corrections)

Major N03. Child kv_block K member never switched to k_vnni_tensor owner

Where
7. Implementation steps and files (child adaptation table)
Round-1 link
touches C04, F12, C04, C04, F39
Claim
Omission (child scope): the child table says the packed-plane accessor returns k_vnni_view<const bf16,64,128>, but no sentence says that detail::kv_block (the per-head K-then-V storage block in kv_cache.hpp) changes its K member from the bf16s array to a k_vnni_tensor owner when k_vnni::layout_on<head_size> (the compile-time VNNI-K gate) is true. At the child head the K member is still the raw SIMD array and k_vnni_plane, set_k_row, set_k_block and get_k_row all reinterpret_cast it. An implementer who ports that code as-is would build the view by casting the array, which Ben's sketch forbids and which the design's own construction rule forbids for the owner. The name of the replacement accessor is also not given.
Design says
page::k Keep native overloads and their !layout_on restrictions. Add a typed packed-plane accessor only for the packed layout. / page::k_vnni_plane Remove the raw-pointer accessor. Its replacement returns k_vnni_view<const bf16,64,128>.
Evidence
kv_cache.hpp@30c4ac82cb:2377-2380 struct alignas(kv_block_alignment) kv_block { ... alignas(kv_block_alignment) bf16s k[page_size][head_size / chunk_size]; (no kv_block hunk in git diff c7844ca2ce 30c4ac82cb -- h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp). kv_cache.hpp@30c4ac82cb:1689-1692 const bf16* k_vnni_plane(...) const noexcept { return reinterpret_cast<const bf16*>(kv_block<geometry>(slot, kv_head).k); }; same cast at :1701-1710 (set_k_row), :1723-1730 (set_k_block), :1737-1743 (get_k_row). Ben comment 5765866077 (facts-r1): "kv_block selecting k_storage via std::conditional_t on k_vnni::layout_on<head_size>" and "The page arena must construct the actual storage type. We should not obtain typed storage by casting an existing SIMD array to vnni_tensor*." design.txt:334 "Do not construct in an accessor or cast an existing SIMD array object into the new owner." design.txt:322 "Replace only the 16-lane V array with the packed owner. Keep native K and 8-lane V as their existing arrays." grep of design.txt: k_vnni_tensor appears only at lines 369 and 385, never inside a kv_block sentence.
Correction from verification
The finding is right as stated. Add: the revision dropped the original design's sentence "Select the K owner and view using the existing conditions on storage geometry" (evidence/plain-english-review.json; r1-findings.md:132, :381), so the revised child section now says less about K storage selection than the round-1 text did. Also note that the construction rule the finding cites (design.txt:334) is written for the parent's V owner; the design should state the same rule for the child's K owner explicitly rather than rely on extension. The recommendation should add that kv_block::k_view()/k_at() (kv_cache.hpp@30c4ac82cbb6:2394-2415) reinterpret_cast k and must be constrained to the array case, since a reinterpret_cast from a class object is ill-formed when instantiated.
The consequence clause "An implementer who ports that code as-is would build the view by casting the array" overstates. The design's rules block every cast route: no public pointer constructor for packed views [design.txt:222], detail::vnni_access restricted to tensor operations and layout-specific kernels [:224], and no cast of an existing SIMD array into the owner [:334]. An implementer who obeys the design therefore cannot cast. The actual defect is narrower: the implementer must infer the kv_block K-member change and the accessor name from four scattered sentences (design.txt:66, 82, 222, 370), and the revision removed the original design's explicit sentence "Select the K owner/view by the existing storage-geometry gate" [plain-english-review.json:1438], replacing it with "Keep k_vnni::layout_on<head_size>, the compile-time layout selector" [design.txt:370], which no longer names the owner. The recommendation stands as written; add that step 5 at design.txt:370 should restore the owner-selection wording. Line citations for set_k_row, set_k_block and get_k_row are approximate ranges; the casts are at kv_cache.hpp@30c4ac82cbb6:1706, :1730 and :1742, plus the copy path at :2111-2114.
Omission (child scope), confirmed and narrowed: the revised child table promises a packed-plane accessor returning k_vnni_view<const bf16,64,128> (design.txt:376-377) but never says that detail::kv_block's K member becomes k_vnni_tensor<page_size, head_size> under k_vnni::layout_on<head_size> (Ben's std::conditional_t selection), that the accessor is kv_block<geometry>(slot, kv_head).k.as_view(), or what the accessor is named. The revision also dropped the original design's sentence "Select the K owner and view using the existing conditions on storage geometry" (original line 492), so the storage selection is now absent rather than implicit, while section 9 marks C04 accepted. Replace the consequence clause "would build the view by casting the array" with: the design's own rules (design.txt:222, :223, :334) forbid every cast or pointer path, so the implementer has no stated legal source for the promised view and must derive the kv_block member swap, and the K owner's sizeof(k) == sizeof(v), 64-byte alignment and trivial default-construction requirements (needed so the parent's placement-new block-array construction at :326-328 covers K) are stated only for V. Recommendation unchanged: add the child-table row as written. Severity minor: child-only, parent mergeability and Ben's acceptance of the parent unaffected, one legal answer reachable from the design plus the cited sketch, one-row fix.
Recommendation
Add a child-table row: "detail::kv_block K member: when k_vnni::layout_on<head_size> holds, the K member is k_vnni_tensor<page_size, head_size> (std::conditional_t with the existing bf16s array). Keep sizeof(k) == sizeof(v), 64-byte alignment and trivial default construction, so the parent's placement-new block-array construction covers it. k_view()/k_at() exist only for the array member. The packed-plane accessor (name it; keep k_vnni_plane or use the sketch's k_packed) returns kv_block<geometry>(slot, kv_head).k.as_view(), never a cast."
Lead's reading
I confirmed the gap in the child source: at 30c4ac82cb the K member of detail::kv_block is still the raw bf16s array, and k_vnni_plane, set_k_row, set_k_block and get_k_row all reinterpret_cast it (kv_cache.hpp:1692, 1706, 1730, 1742). The revised child table promises a k_vnni_view accessor but never says the member becomes the k_vnni_tensor owner, which is exactly the std::conditional_t selection in Ben's sketch and the construction rule the design itself states for V. One verifier graded minor because the design's other rules would block a cast route; I keep major because the child adaptation is the reason the parent exists, and this is its central sentence.
Found by
child-needs, ben-fidelity, simplicity-scope; 3 of 3 verifiers could not refute it (votes and corrections)

Major N04. Definition of done: no record location or gating for reviewer answers

Where
8. Verification and acceptance (Definition of done) and 2. Review request
Round-1 link
touches F02
Claim
Ambiguity: the definition of done requires the reviewer questions to have recorded answers, but the design names no place where answers are recorded, no owner who records them, and no rule for what the implementer does if the reviewer has not answered when implementation starts. Section 2 says the questions remain open and need no answer now, and section 2 defers the PR reply to the future, so the implementer cannot tell whether to wait, to proceed on the Proposed answer column, or to post the questions.
Design says
The reviewer questions have recorded answers, and the implemented interface matches them.
Evidence
design.txt:11 'the reviewer questions remain open before implementation'; design.txt:78 'They are proposals, not recorded reviewer approval. The document revision does not require answering them now.'; design.txt:89 'A future PR reply should link issue #4525 and these questions. Minor M01. t_llama_unit itself does not compile in the 8-lane configuration at unmodified main

Where
8. Verification and acceptance (Build configurations; Behavior checks rows that name t_llama_unit); 5. Build and include boundaries
Round-1 link
touches N61, N01, F47, F11
Claim
Factual gap plus ambiguity. The 8-lane row tells the implementing agent to build and run the t_llama_unit cases (t_llama_unit is the cache and model unit-test program) under TRON_CHUNK_SIZE == 8 (the AVX2 build with 8 fp32 values per vector). At live main that program cannot compile in that build, for two reasons independent of the parent. First, the template check_wide_scaled_v calls page::set_v, and its two instantiations at the 'scaled_v supports wide values' case are not guarded; page::set_v is declared only under #if TRON_CHUNK_SIZE == 16. Second, the requires-expression constant page_supports_uniform_kv_access asks for page.get_v with a bf16* destination and is asserted true with STATIC_REQUIRE on the empty uniform cache; get_v also exists only in the 16-lane build. The file guards five other set_v/get_v sites, so the two unguarded sites look like an oversight on main, not intent. Round-2 finding N61 records the same problem for t_heterogeneous_scheduler only. The consequence for the design: the 8-lane row cannot execute or compile the behavior rows it names, and N01's remedy (make the 8-lane row compile-only) still cannot include t_llama_unit. The one 8-lane target that does exercise the new header is t_amx_dispatch_dtype, which includes tron/models/model.hpp and tron/plugins/llama.hpp and therefore kv_cache.hpp. The darwin preset (macOS x86_64 cross-compile) is the only preset with AVX512 OFF, so an 8-lane build is a supported configuration, not a hypothetical one. I did not compile anything; the claim is inferred from the declarations, as N61's was.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
8-lane, AMX code off cmake --preset native -DAVX512=OFF -DTRON_AMX_DISPATCH=OFF No reviewed required or manual CI lane covers this configuration. Establish the unmodified-baseline result before attributing a failure to the parent. Include unconditional fake AMX definitions.

Minor M02. fill_storage_slot: a whole-block raw V writer the search pattern and the raw-access rule both miss

Where
5. Interface design (Raw storage access row); 7. Implementation steps (step 2, step 6, caller search at the end of the table)
Round-1 link
touches F10, C06, F01
Claim
Omission. The design says detail::vnni_access (the private friend that hands out the packed plane pointer) is the only raw access, and its caller search uses the names v_base, v_data, set_v, get_v, append_v and scaled_v. book::fill_storage_slot (a test-only helper called by book::fill_random) matches none of those names. It reinterpret_casts a whole kv_block (the per-head K-then-V storage block) to bf16* and writes every element, including the V plane, by index. Its comment says it bypasses the zero-initialized V invariant on purpose. After the parent, that loop writes through the packed owner's object representation from outside the type. The implementing agent has to decide whether that stays (a byte fill of a trivially copyable block is legal and keeps the test's intent) or moves into the owner, and the design gives no rule and would not find the site with its own search. t_llama_unit's fixed-layout append case depends on fill_random.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
detail::vnni_access is the internal friend used only by tensor operations and layout-specific kernels. It is an implementation boundary, not a security boundary.
h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1365-1374 (void fill_storage_slot(kv_storage_offset offset, Distribution& distribution, Gen& gen) noexcept { ... auto& block = kv_block_at_storage<geometry>(offset, kv_head, pg); bf16* data = reinterpret_cast<bf16*>(&block); for (size_t i = 0; i < sizeof(block) / sizeof(bf16); ++i) data[i] = static_cast<bf16>(distribution(gen)); }), :1055-1065 (// For testing only. Writes raw bf16 data that bypasses the zero-init V slot invariant ... for_each_active_slot(... fill_storage_slot<geometry> ...); if (has_eagle_storage()) fill_storage_slot<layout_t::primary_geometry>(eagle_storage_offset(), ...)). t/t_llama_unit.cpp@2880c3aa9b:783-784 (destination.fill_random(gen); source.fill_random(gen);). design.txt:223 (Raw storage access row), :360 (rg pattern), :450 ("Packed physical addresses are confined to tensor operations and layout-specific kernels"); grep of design.txt for fill_random|fill_storage_slot: no match.
Correction from verification
Three wording fixes, none changing the verdict. (a) The design does not literally say detail::vnni_access "is the only raw access"; design.txt:223 says it "is the internal friend used only by tensor operations and layout-specific kernels", and :450 says packed physical addresses are confined to those places. Reword the claim to: the design confines packed V byte access to tensor operations and layout-specific kernels and names no exception for the test-only whole-block fill. (b) t_llama_unit.cpp@2880c3aa9b lines are 784-785, not 783-784, and a second dependent exists: "apply and join page ranges" at :2008 (bk.fill_random(gen);), so the recommendation should name both cases. (c) "a byte fill of a trivially copyable block is legal" should read "compiles unchanged and is no worse than main": the K plane is already bf16s (= bf16x16, h/tron/simd/auto.hpp@2880c3aa9b:54) written through bf16* today, so the parent neither introduces nor removes the aliasing question; the parent's requirements at design.txt:328 (trivially copyable block, same byte size) keep the loop well-formed.
Omission (narrowed). The revised design never mentions book::fill_random / book::fill_storage_slot (kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1055-1065, 1364-1374), the test-only helper that fills every bf16 of a whole kv_block, including the V plane, through reinterpret_cast<bf16*>(&block), used by t/t_llama_unit.cpp:784-785 and :2008. It calls no removed accessor, so the rg search at design.txt:360 ("before removing accessors") rightly need not list it; it computes no packed physical address, so the definition-of-done sentence at design.txt:450 is not contradicted; and the design's own requirements at design.txt:328 (trivial owner and block, unchanged block size, offsets and alignment) mean the fill stays valid and compiles unchanged. What is missing is one sentence saying so, because the encapsulation rules at design.txt:210 (owner stores no pointer to its array) and :226 (no pointer conversion for packed views) otherwise leave an implementing agent to guess whether a raw whole-block write from outside the type may remain. Recommended fix: add one row to the "Existing callers and their replacements" table: "book::fill_random / fill_storage_slot (test-only): unchanged; layout-blind whole-block fill of a trivially copyable block that intentionally bypasses the partner-zero rule; keep its comment's reference to Note [Zero-Initialized V Slots] when that Note is updated under C06." Do not add the names to the rg pattern; do not move the fill into the owner.
Three small corrections to the finding's details, none of which weaken it: (1) the t_llama_unit.cpp caller is at lines 784-785, not 783-784, and fill_random has a second caller at t/t_llama_unit.cpp:2008 in TEST_CASE "apply and join page ranges" (line 1953), so two test cases depend on it; (2) the phrase "the design says detail::vnni_access ... is the only raw access" overstates: design.txt:223 says vnni_access is "the internal friend used only by tensor operations and layout-specific kernels" and design.txt:450 confines "packed physical addresses" to those places; fill_storage_slot computes no packed address, so a careful agent would not read those sentences as forbidding it — the defect is that the caller search (design.txt:360) and the accessor outcome table (design.txt:252-256) omit the site, and no sentence says whether it stays; (3) fill_storage_slot is in book's private section (after private: at kv_cache.hpp:1274), so the public entry point the agent will meet is book::fill_random (kv_cache.hpp:1058); the recommended table row should name fill_random first and fill_storage_slot as its helper.
Recommendation
Add to the callers table: "book::fill_storage_slot / fill_random (test-only, kv_cache.hpp:1055-1065, 1365-1374): keep as a byte fill of the whole trivially copyable block; it intentionally bypasses the partner-zero rule and stays outside the typed operations. Say so in its comment." Add fill_random|fill_storage_slot to the search at design.txt:360, and state in the definition of done that this test-only fill is the one raw whole-block writer allowed outside detail::vnni_access.

Minor M03. Compile-rejection rows name no mechanism; the get_v concept check must be rewritten

Where
8. Verification and acceptance (Typed function boundaries row; Expression reads row); 7. step 6; 5. Existing callers table (page::get_v row)
Round-1 link
touches F10, F38, C03
Claim
Ambiguity that makes the implementing agent guess. The 'Typed function boundaries' row asks for compile checks that reject wrong types but does not say how a Catch2 test expresses 'this call must not compile'. The repository precedent is a requires-expression bound to a constexpr bool, asserted with STATIC_REQUIRE or STATIC_REQUIRE_FALSE; t_llama_unit already does this for the page and book interfaces in the case 'attention operations resolve to typed KV slots'. AGENTS.md asks to extend the closest existing test, so that case and those constants are the place. The same constants are also affected callers: page_supports_uniform_kv_access encodes get_v with a bf16* destination and is asserted true on the uniform page and false on the heterogeneous page. The get_v row says 'Wrap or accept a typed destination', so an agent who chooses 'wrap' (view parameter only) turns the true assertion into a compile failure and must rewrite the constant with a view argument; an agent who chooses 'accept' keeps a raw-pointer overload the design elsewhere removes. The design should pick one and name the constant.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
Typed function boundaries: compile checks in an existing required suite Reject raw pointers and packed V at native-K kernel calls; reject native K at packed-V calls. Reject read-only writes, const-row mutation, reverse const conversion, and rvalue-owner views. Accept writable-to-read-only conversion.
design.txt:397 (Typed function boundaries row), :253 (page::get_v row), :357 (step 6). t/t_llama_unit.cpp@2880c3aa9b:84-123 (requires-expression constants: page_k_accepts_raw_index :84-86, page_supports_uniform_kv_access :105-109 with bf16* output, page_k_accepts_geometry :111-113, page_scaled_v_accepts_geometry :119-123), :316 (TEST_CASE("attention operations resolve to typed KV slots", "[kv_data]")), :327-342 (STATIC_REQUIRE_FALSE block, e.g. :338 STATIC_REQUIRE_FALSE(page_k_accepts_raw_index<page_t>);), :483 (STATIC_REQUIRE(page_supports_uniform_kv_access<empty_cache_t::page_t>);), :779 (STATIC_REQUIRE_FALSE(page_supports_uniform_kv_access<page_t>);). AGENTS.md@2880c3aa9b:50-52 ("Prefer extending the closest existing test with the smallest case").
Correction from verification
Corrected claim: design.txt:397 asks for "compile checks in an existing required suite" without naming the mechanism, the case, or the constants, although t/t_llama_unit.cpp@2880c3aa9b already expresses such checks as requires-expression constants (:84-123) asserted with STATIC_REQUIRE / STATIC_REQUIRE_FALSE (:338-342 in the case "attention operations resolve to typed KV slots", :316). One of those constants, page_supports_uniform_kv_access (:105-109), calls get_v with a bf16* output; it is asserted true at :483 and false at :779. design.txt:253 leaves get_v as "Wrap or accept a typed destination": if get_v keeps the bf16* parameter and wraps it into a view internally ("wrap", matching design.txt:242 "Wrap caller-owned scratch in an existing contiguous view"), the constant and both assertions are unchanged; if get_v's parameter becomes the typed view ("accept", matching set_v at :250), the constant becomes false and STATIC_REQUIRE at :483 fails to compile, so the constant must be rewritten with a view argument. The design does not remove a raw-pointer get_v anywhere (design.txt:271 and :397 concern kernel declarations and kernel calls; :377 concerns the child's k_vnni_plane), so the "accept" branch does not contradict another design statement; it only leaves a decision that round-1 F10 asked for and that section 9 (design.txt:463) reports as closed. Recommendation unchanged: name the mechanism and the case in the :397 row, pick one get_v form in the :253 row, and if the typed-view form is chosen, say that page_supports_uniform_kv_access (:105-109) must be rewritten so :483 and :779 keep their truth values. Citation fix: the AGENTS.md quote is at 2880c3aa9b:53-54, not :50-52.
Ambiguity (design.txt:253, get_v row; :397, Typed function boundaries row). The revised design leaves get_v's destination form as "Wrap or accept a typed destination" and does not list it among the reviewer questions; it also names no mechanism or test case for the compile-rejection checks. Corrected consequence: if the implementer keeps the raw bf16*/float* destination and wraps it inside get_v ("wrap"), page_supports_uniform_kv_access (t/t_llama_unit.cpp@2880c3aa9b:105-109) compiles unchanged and :483/:779 keep their truth values; if the implementer changes the parameter to a typed view ("accept"), the requires-expression becomes false, STATIC_REQUIRE at :483 fails at build time, and the constant plus full.hpp:2652-2656 and six t_llama_unit call sites (:819, :1575, :2038, :2539, :2761, :2824) must be rewritten. The design does not elsewhere remove a raw destination pointer on get_v (its raw-pointer removals are scoped to kernel cache-plane arguments, design.txt:262/:270, and packed-view constructors, :221). Recommended text: (a) in the get_v row, replace "Wrap or accept" with one choice; the design's own load_row bullet (:242 "Wrap caller-owned scratch in an existing contiguous view") and the kept v_head_fn callback shape (:253) favor "keep the bf16* and float* destination parameters, wrap inside, call load_row", which leaves page_supports_uniform_kv_access untouched; if the typed-view form is chosen instead, name the constant and the seven call sites to rewrite; (b) in the Typed function boundaries row, add "Express each rejection as a requires-expression constant plus STATIC_REQUIRE_FALSE next to the existing constants at t_llama_unit.cpp:84-123, inside the case 'attention operations resolve to typed KV slots' (:316-342)."
Ambiguity that makes the implementing agent guess. The "Typed function boundaries" row (design.txt:397) asks for compile checks in an existing required suite but names neither the mechanism nor the case; the repository precedent is a requires-expression constant asserted with STATIC_REQUIRE / STATIC_REQUIRE_FALSE in t/t_llama_unit.cpp@2880c3aa9b:84-123 and :316-342 (case "attention operations resolve to typed KV slots"), and AGENTS.md@2880c3aa9b:53-54 asks to extend the closest existing test. The get_v row (design.txt:253) leaves two forms open, "Wrap or accept a typed destination", while the set_v row (:252) decides on a typed view. The forms differ in consequence: keeping the bf16*/float* parameters and wrapping internally ("wrap") leaves page_supports_uniform_kv_access (:105-109, bf16* output) and its assertions at :483 (true) and :779 (false) unchanged; changing the parameter to a view ("accept") makes the constant false on the uniform page, fails :483 at build time, and requires rewriting the constant plus the six raw-pointer test callers (:819, :1575, :2038, :2539, :2761, :2824) and full.hpp:2651-2657. Recommendation

Minor M04. Heterogeneous construction pass: the compile-time geometry dispatch helper is unnamed although book already has one

Where
6. Behavior the implementation must preserve (Arena construction without clearing, heterogeneous bullet); 7. step 2
Round-1 link
touches C09, F12, C01
Claim
Ambiguity. The heterogeneous bullet says to use the slot specifications to choose each region's compile-time head width and head count, but the geometry is a template argument of kv_block_t and the slot specifications are a constexpr array, so the agent needs a compile-time loop that hands each slot index its geometry as a template argument. book already has exactly that: for_each_active_slot, built on for_each_slot_impl over std::index_sequence, calls a generic lambda with <size_t slot, kv_geometry geometry> for every active logical slot. fill_random is the precedent: it runs for_each_active_slot and then handles the EAGLE slot separately with the primary geometry. The design names neither, so an agent may write a second index_sequence dispatch. Two facts that make the helper usable from inside allocate_kv_group, where the design places the construction, are also unstated: storage_slot_count_ is set in the constructor's initializer list before allocate_kv_group runs, so logical_slot_count() and slot_base are valid there; and slot_base returns nullptr for an arena not yet allocated, so the retained-arena pass (which runs before the reclaimable arena exists) must select slots by layout_t::storage_location(...).reclaimable, as the bullet says, not by a null check alone.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
For heterogeneous geometry, enumerate physical storage offsets once and select only slots in the just-allocated arena. Restoring the reclaimable arena must not restart objects in the retained arena. Use the slot specifications to choose each region's compile-time head width and head count.
h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1236-1250 (template <size_t... slots, typename F> static constexpr void for_each_slot_impl(std::index_sequence<slots...>, F&& function) { (function.template operator()<slots, kv_slot_specs[slots].geometry>(), ...); } and void for_each_active_slot(F&& function) const { size_t const active_slots = logical_slot_count(); ... if (slot < active_slots) function.template operator()<slot, geometry>(); }), :1055-1065 (fill_random precedent), :1090-1094 (constructor initializer list sets storage_slot_count_(storage_slot_count)), :1101-1102 (allocate_kv_group(false, retained_kv_bytes_) || allocate_kv_group(true, reclaimable_kv_bytes_)), :1188-1197 (slot_base: if (!arena) return nullptr;). design.txt:332, :334 ("Run the construction helper in book::allocate_kv_group after installing a successful allocation"); grep of design.txt for for_each_active_slot: no match.
Correction from verification
Keep the claim, with two edits. (1) Replace the null-check sentence with: "A null test on slot_base selects correctly only in the retained pass (the reclaimable arena does not exist yet, kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1192). In the reclaimable pass (constructor second call :1102 and restore_reclaimable_kv_storage :968) the retained arena is present, so retained slots also return non-null and would be constructed again. Filter by layout_t::storage_location(offset, logical_slot_count()).reclaimable == reclaimable (:727-735) in both passes." (2) Add: "The design alludes to the helper without naming it: design.txt:333 'the logical active-slot loop'. Name it." Cite slot_base's null return as :1192, not :1193.
Keep the finding as minor with these corrections. (a) Claim wording: the design refers to the helper obliquely ("the logical active-slot loop", design.txt:333) but never names for_each_active_slot, for_each_slot_impl or fill_random, and none of its kv_cache.hpp links (L1187-L1206, L1274-L1334, L946-L981) reaches lines 1055-1065 or 1236-1249; round-1 C09's verifier corrections asked for exactly this naming and the "Accepted" row at design.txt:468 did not adopt it. (b) Null-check reasoning: during the retained pass (first allocate_kv_group call, kv_cache.hpp:1101) the reclaimable arena is absent, so slot_base returns nullptr for reclaimable slots (:1193) and a null check would skip them by accident. The hazard is the reclaimable pass (:1102) and restore_reclaimable_kv_storage (:968): both arenas exist then, so a null check selects every slot and re-constructs the retained arena's objects, which design.txt:332 forbids ("Restoring the reclaimable arena must not restart objects in the retained arena"). Select by layout_t::storage_location(offset, logical_slot_count()).reclaimable == reclaimable, as the design already says. (c) Validity inside allocate_kv_group: both storage_slot_count_ and support_eagle_ are set in the initializer list (:1091-1093), and has_eagle_storage() returns support_eagle_ (:925-927), so logical_slot_count() (:920-922) is already correct there. (d) EAGLE: the heterogeneous per-slot pass never meets the EAGLE slot, because EAGLE requires uniform geometry (:771, :1149-1151) and the design constructs uniform arenas as one whole-arena array (design.txt:331). So for_each_active_slot alone is complete for the heterogeneous pass; the EAGLE branch of fill_random is needed only if the agent chooses per-slot construction for uniform layouts too. Recommended sentence for the bullet: "Reuse book::for_each_active_slot (kv_cache.hpp:1242-1249), which calls a lambda with <slot, geometry> template arguments as fill_random does (:1058-1065); inside it keep only slots whose storage_location(offset, logical_slot_count()).reclaimable equals the arena being constructed, and construct geometry.n_kv_heads * n_pages blocks at slot_base(offset). Do not select slots by a null slot_base: in the reclaimable pass and in restore both arenas exist."
Three corrections, none changing the verdict. (a) "The design names neither" overstates: design.txt:333 refers to the helper descriptively ("Construction cannot rely solely on the logical active-slot loop, which omits that extra physical slot") but only as something insufficient for EAGLE; it never tells the agent to use it for the heterogeneous pass. Restate as: the design mentions the loop only as a caution and does not name for_each_active_slot or fill_random as the thing to reuse. (b) The null-check hazard is in the wrong pass. During the retained pass the reclaimable arena is absent, so slot_base returns nullptr for reclaimable slots [kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1192] and a null check would skip them correctly by accident. The hazard is the reclaimable pass (initial at :1102, restore at :968): both arenas exist, a null check selects every slot, and the retained arena would be re-constructed. The design already forbids this at design.txt:332 ("layout_t::storage_location identifies its arena"; "must not restart objects in the retained arena"), so this sub-item needs no design change; drop it or keep it as a note. (c) Line nits: the null check is at :1192 (finding says :1193); storage_slot_count_ is initialized at :1093 (finding says :1094). Recommended design text stays as the finding proposes, minus the null-check sentence: "Use book::for_each_active_slot (kv_cache.hpp:1242-1249), which calls a lambda with <slot, geometry> for each active logical slot, as fill_random does (:1057-1065); inside it keep only slots whose layout_t::storage_location(kv_storage_offset(slot), logical_slot_count()).reclaimable equals the arena just allocated, then default-construct geometry.n_kv_heads * n_pages blocks at slot_base(kv_storage_offset(slot))."
Recommendation
Add to the bullet: "Use book::for_each_active_slot (kv_cache.hpp:1243-1250), which calls a lambda with <slot, geometry> for each active logical slot, as fill_random does (:1057-1065); inside it, keep only slots whose layout_t::storage_location(offset, logical_slot_count()).reclaimable equals the arena being constructed, then construct geometry.n_kv_heads * n_pages blocks at slot_base(offset). storage_slot_count_ is already set when allocate_kv_group runs (:1094), so these calls are valid there. Do not test slot_base for null to select slots: the reclaimable arena is absent during the retained pass (:1193)."

Minor N09. Q3 omits reviewer's reason for excluding operator[]

Where
2. Questions for the reviewer before implementation (Q3)
Round-1 link
touches F02
Claim
Omission. The reviewer's sketch excludes operator[] for a stated reason: it "suggests a contiguous row". Question 3 asks whether the view may provide operator[] but states neither that reason nor the design's rebuttal, which section 5 already contains: the returned v_vnni_row (the logical-row expression type) has no data pointer, does not inherit views<>, and is marked dma = false, so it does not claim contiguous storage. Without the reason and the rebuttal in the question, the reviewer cannot see that his concern is met; the round-1 F02 verifier asked for exactly these sentences.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
May the view provide operator[] as well as at(token, dimension)? Yes. The expression interface requires row indexing. Retain at() as an equivalent bounds-checked spelling.
Ben comment 5765866077: "There is no data(), pointer conversion, or operator[] that suggests a contiguous row." design.txt:84 (Q3); design.txt:212 "Set dma = false: logical row values are not consecutive in memory"; design.txt:213 "Do not inherit views<>, whose leaf rows must be contiguous"; design.txt:222 "No public pointer constructor, .data member, or pointer conversion for packed views." r1-findings F02, second verifier correction: "the sketch avoids operator[] because it suggests a contiguous row. The expr base requires operator[] [h/tron/kernels/expr.hpp:57-65 @f46e48bab498]".
Correction from verification
Two wording fixes to the finding's rebuttal text; the omission itself stands. (1) The row is not pointer-free. design.txt:212 says "The row stores its pair address and even/odd position by value", so v_vnni_row (the proposed logical-row type) does hold a pointer into packed storage. What the design guarantees is narrower [design.txt:222]: "No public pointer constructor, .data member, or pointer conversion for packed views." The recommended Q3 answer should say "exposes no .data member or pointer conversion", not "has no data pointer". (2) dma = false is not by itself a non-contiguity claim. views.hpp:11-15 @2880c3aa9b documents the dma flag as DMA-safety ("Logically, being DMA-safe requires contiguity ... but we track that separately"). The rebuttal that carries the weight is "Do not inherit views<>, whose leaf rows must be contiguous" [design.txt:213] plus the no-pointer-exposure rule. Corrected proposed answer: "Yes. The sketch excluded operator[] because it suggests a contiguous row. The expression base expr<B,R,D> forwards operator[] to the derived type [h/tron/kernels/expr.hpp:57-65 @f46e48bab498], so the matrix provides operator[] returning v_vnni_row, a logical row that does not inherit views<> and exposes no .data member or pointer conversion; it does not claim contiguous storage. at(token, dimension) stays as a bounds-asserted single-element spelling."
Two precision fixes to the finding's recommended text, neither changing the verdict. (1) Do not use "dma = false" as the contiguity disclaimer. h/tron/tensor/views.hpp:11-15 @f46e48bab498 defines dma as "the view refers to DMA-safe memory" and says "being DMA-safe requires contiguity, namely that the last stride is 1, but we track that separately." The accurate rebuttal is: the row has no data pointer and no .data member (design.txt:212, 222) and the matrix view does not derive from views<>, whose leaf rows must have stride 1 (views.hpp:47-51; design.txt:213). The design's own line 212 has the same conflation (noted in facts-r2.md), so the Q3 rewrite should not repeat it. (2) "does not inherit views<>" applies to the matrix view v_vnni_view (design.txt:213), not to v_vnni_row, which inherits expr<row_type,D> only (design.txt:211). A rank-1 view never inherits views<> in the repository either (view.hpp:115-117 @f46e48bab498 inherits expr directly; only the rank-2+ view at view.hpp:25-31 conditionally inherits views<>), so saying the row "has no views<> base" is trivially true and not the distinguishing point. Suggested Q3 answer: "Yes. The sketch excluded operator[] because it suggests a contiguous row. The expression base expr<B,R,D> forwards operator[] to the derived type (expr.hpp:56-65), so the matrix provides operator[] returning v_vnni_row, a logical row that stores a pair address and parity by value and exposes no data pointer; the matrix view does not derive from views<>, whose leaf rows must be contiguous. at(token, dimension) stays as a bounds-asserted single-element spelling."
Finding stands. Adjust its rebuttal wording: replace "has no data pointer ... and is marked dma = false, so it does not claim contiguous storage" with "exposes no public data() member or pointer conversion [design.txt:222] and does not inherit views<>, whose leaf rows must be stride-1 contiguous [views.hpp:46-50 @f46e48bab498]; dma = false is a conservative flag, not a contiguity statement [views.hpp:11-16]". Suggested Q3 answer: "Yes. The sketch excluded operator[] because it suggests a contiguous row. The expression base expr<B,R,D> (expr.hpp:57-65) requires operator[] returning a row expression, so the matrix provides operator[] returning v_vnni_row, a logical row with no public data() or pointer conversion and no views<> base, so it does not claim contiguous storage. at(token, dimension) stays as a bounds-asserted single-element spelling. This differs from the sketch."
Recommendation
Rewrite the proposed answer: "Yes. The sketch excluded operator[] because it suggests a contiguous row. The expression base expr<B,R,D> (expr.hpp:57-65) requires operator[] returning a row expression, so the matrix provides operator[] returning v_vnni_row, a logical row with no data pointer, no views<> base and dma = false; it does not claim contiguous storage. at(token, dimension) stays as a bounds-asserted single-element spelling."

Minor N13. bf16_chunk 'different signature silently selects conversion' is wrong

Where
5. Interface design (operation table) and 8. Verification (Expression reads row)
Round-1 link
touches F35
Claim
Factual error: the revision repeats the claim that a different bf16_chunk signature silently selects conversion. The expr base (expr.hpp, tron's expression interface) decides by comparing member-function pointers: if constexpr (&B::bf16_chunk != &expr::bf16_chunk). Only a MISSING override takes the fallback silently. A mismatched const qualifier, parameter type, return type, an overload set, or a template makes &B::bf16_chunk ill-formed or incomparable, which is a compile error. A missing noexcept still compares unequal and the override is used. Factual error: section 8 calls the fallback lossy. The fallback fp32s_to_bf16s(chunk(i)) truncates and only changes bits when the exponent is saturated and the low 16 bits are nonzero; after even_part (shift left 16) or odd_part (mask high 16) the low 16 bits are always zero, so the fallback is bit-exact for stored bf16 and only costs extra instructions. The section-8 check 'exact bf16 copies with independent patterns' therefore cannot detect an omitted override. Round-1 F35's verifier corrections said both of these; the revised handling of F35 kept the wrong sentence.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
bf16s bf16_chunk(size_t i) const noexcept Use this exact non-template override signature. Extract stored bf16 bits directly. The expression base detects the override through its member function; a different signature can silently select conversion instead. [...] The new type cannot be consumed by expr, or silently takes a lossy fallback.
h/tron/kernels/expr.hpp:110-117@2880c3aa9b (bf16s bf16_chunk(size_t i) const noexcept { if constexpr (&B::bf16_chunk != &expr::bf16_chunk) return self().bf16_chunk(i); else return fp32s_to_bf16s(self().chunk(i)); }); h/tron/simd/bf16.hpp:92-109@f46e48ba (fp32s_to_bf16s: OR 0x00010000 only where exponent == 0x7f800000 AND low 16 bits != 0, then srli 16); h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp:2000-2007@2880c3aa9b (odd_part = and hi_16_of_32, even_part = slli_epi32 16: low 16 bits zero); design.txt:221 and :398; r1-findings.md F35 verifier corrections ('Silent fallback happens only when vnni_row declares no bf16_chunk at all ... a compile error, not a silent fallback'; 'bit-exact for data that came from bf16').
Correction from verification
Replace "the revision repeats the claim" with "the revision introduces the claim" (the original design row at design-text.txt:260 had neither the "different signature" sentence nor "lossy fallback"). Qualify the noexcept sub-claim: per the C++ standard a missing noexcept compiles and the override is used, but g++ 11.4 rejects the comparison as distinct pointer types; clang 19 (the tron toolchain) was not tested here. In both cases the result is a compile-time outcome, never a silent fallback.
Two wording corrections to the finding; the substance stands. (1) "the revision repeats the claim" and "kept the wrong sentence" are inaccurate. The original design (/tmp/issue4525-design-before.html, 67,216 bytes = the round-1 size in facts-r2.md) contains zero occurrences of "lossy", "silently" or "different signature"; its bf16_chunk row read "Extract the stored bf16 bits directly. Avoid a float conversion during exact copies." Both defective sentences [design.txt:221, :398] were INTRODUCED by the revision while handling F35, as member R90's title says. (2) "A missing noexcept still compares unequal and the override is used" is what the standard specifies (function pointer conversion of the noexcept member pointer in the equality comparison), but g++ 11.4 rejects that comparison as "distinct pointer types", and the pinned clang 19 behaviour was not verified here. Either outcome is loud; neither is a silent fallback, so the correction to the design is unchanged: only an omitted (or differently named) bf16_chunk silently selects fp32s_to_bf16s(chunk(i)), which is bit-exact for stored bf16 and costs a shift, a NaN check and a narrow per chunk; any other signature fails to compile at the first bf16 consumer. Section 8 should name the static_assert on decltype(&v_vnni_row<...>::bf16_chunk) or the objdump record of the bf16 read region as the detection, not a bit-pattern test.
Hedge one sub-claim: replace "A missing noexcept still compares unequal and the override is used" with "A missing noexcept is either accepted as the override (C++17 composite pointer type rule) or rejected at compile time (g++ 11.4 rejects the derived/base plus noexcept-only difference; clang 19, tron's toolchain, untested here); it is never a silent fallback." Everything else in the finding holds as written.
Recommendation
Replace the contract text with: 'expr<B,N>::bf16_chunk compares member-function pointers (&B::bf16_chunk != &expr::bf16_chunk, expr.hpp:112). Declare exactly one non-template, non-overloaded bf16s bf16_chunk(size_t) const noexcept in v_vnni_row. Omitting it silently selects fp32s_to_bf16s(chunk(i)); that fallback is bit-exact for stored bf16 but adds a shift, NaN check, and narrow per chunk. Guard the override with static_assert(std::is_same_v<decltype(&v_vnni_row<const bf16, 128>::bf16_chunk), bf16s (v_vnni_row<const bf16, 128>::*)(size_t) const noexcept>) in vnni.hpp.' In section 8, change 'silently takes a lossy fallback' to 'silently takes the fp32 conversion fallback (bit-exact, slower)' and name the static_assert or the instruction record, not a bit-pattern test, as the detection.
Lead's reading
The verifiers agree on the mechanism: expr.hpp compares member-function pointers, so a wrong signature is a compile error and only a missing override falls back silently. They also agree the fallback is bit-exact for stored bf16 (the low 16 bits are zero after even_part or odd_part), so section 8's word lossy is wrong and a bit-pattern test cannot detect the omission. The revision introduced this sentence; the original design did not have it.

cpp-interface, dispo:B06, handoff-actionability, formulas-and-page; 3 of 3 verifiers could not refute it (votes and corrections)

Minor N14. native_k_view alias relies on Stride default; header unstated

Where
5. Interface design (declarations block, kernel signatures, Build and include boundaries)
Round-1 link
touches F43, F11
Claim
Ambiguity that makes the implementer guess: the alias native_k_view<Rows,Cols> = view<bf16, true, false, seq<Rows, Cols>> has four template arguments and relies on the fifth parameter's default Stride = row_major<Dim>. That default is declared only in h/tron/tensor/views.hpp (the header that defines tron's view primary template), which pulls in expr.hpp and the SIMD intrinsics headers. The same section tells the implementer to keep amx_attn_iface.hpp (the AMX kernel interface header) intrinsics-free, to forward-declare the five-parameter view/const_view templates, and not to repeat template defaults. A forward declaration without the default cannot supply it, so the kernel declaration qk_rowmajor_128x4(const_native_k_view<PAGE_TOKENS_64, HEAD_SIZE_128> k_page, ...) as printed cannot be written in that header. The two spellings the design offers (std::integer_sequence or a shared declarations header) both require the stride to be written out. Round-1 F43's verifiers asked for exactly this (stride spelled as sseq<Cols,1>); the revised alias text did not change. The resulting type is the same as page::k / kv_block::k_view return today, so the risk is a compile error and a guess, not a wrong build.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
using native_k_view = view<bf16, true, false, seq<Rows, Cols>>; [...] using const_native_k_view = const_view<bf16, true, false, seq<Rows, Cols>>; [...] Forward-declare the packed view and the existing five-parameter view/const_view templates. Spell native dimensions and strides with std::integer_sequence, or share a small intrinsics-free declarations header. [...] Do not repeat template defaults.
h/tron/tensor/views.hpp:22-27@2880c3aa9b (template <typename T, bool aligned, bool dma, typename Dim, typename Stride = row_major<Dim>> struct view) and :31-36 (const_view with the same default); h/tron/kernels/expr.hpp:17-22@2880c3aa9b (forward declarations of view/const_view WITHOUT the default: the precedent the design follows); h/tron/tensor/seq.hpp:21-25, 393-394@2880c3aa9b (seq, sseq, row_major; seq.hpp includes only common/assert.hpp, common/attributes.hpp, common/numerics/std.hpp, none of which contain intrinsics per grep); h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp:2208-2219@2880c3aa9b (k_view returns view<bf16, true, false, seq<page_size, head_size>> / const_view<...>, i.e. the defaulted stride sseq<head_size,1>); design.txt:203-205, :261, :272-273; r1-findings.md F43 verifier correction ('the alias const_native_k_view needs seq.hpp included and the stride spelled out (sseq<HEAD_SIZE_128,1>)').
Correction from verification
The design already instructs the implementer to spell dimensions and strides explicitly inside amx_attn_iface.hpp (design.txt:272) and records that intent in section 9 (design.txt:475), so the claim "the kernel declaration as printed cannot be written in that header" applies to the alias and snippet as printed, not to the design's stated method. The residual defects are three: (1) the stride value is never given (row_major<seq<Rows,Cols>> resolves to sseq<Cols,1>, seq.hpp:379-394@2880c3aa9b); (2) no header is named as the single definition site of native_k_view and const_native_k_view, and steps 1-4 (design.txt:352-355) do not place them; (3) the kernel snippet at design.txt:261 uses the four-argument alias inside a declaration that step 4 places in amx_attn_iface.hpp, which contradicts :272 for that header. The recommendation stands: write the two aliases with five arguments (sseq<Cols, 1> as Stride), name the intrinsics-free declarations header that defines them (including tron/tensor/seq.hpp and forward-declaring view, const_view and v_vnni_view), say amx_attn_iface.hpp includes it, and add one sentence that the alias equals the type kv_block::k_view returns (kv_cache.hpp:2210, 2217@2880c3aa9b).
The finding is right; one addition strengthens it. The design's option 1 ("Spell native dimensions and strides with std::integer_sequence", design.txt:272) is a consistent route only if the element types are also stated: dims are std::integer_sequence<size_t, ...> (seq, seq.hpp:21-22) and strides are std::integer_sequence<ptrdiff_t, ...> (sseq, seq.hpp:24-25). A stride written as std::integer_sequence<size_t, 128, 1> names a different const_view type from the one page::k / kv_block::k_view return (kv_cache.hpp:2215-2219, default row_major<seq<64,128>> = sseq<128,1>), and the call site then fails to compile. The recommendation should therefore say: write the fifth argument as sseq<Cols, 1> (or row_major<seq<Rows, Cols>>, which seq.hpp:393-394 provides intrinsics-free), and name the one header that defines the two aliases exactly once.
Recommendation
Change the two aliases to five arguments: using native_k_view = view<bf16, true, false, seq<Rows, Cols>, sseq<Cols, 1>>; and the const twin (sseq<Cols,1> equals row_major<seq<Rows,Cols>>). State where they live: a small intrinsics-free declarations header that includes tron/tensor/seq.hpp, forward-declares template <typename T, bool aligned, bool dma, typename Dim, typename Stride> struct view; and struct const_view; (five parameters, no default, as expr.hpp:17-22 does), and forward-declares template <typename T, size_t Rows, size_t Cols> class v_vnni_view;. amx_attn_iface.hpp includes that header. Add one sentence: the alias is the exact type page::k / kv_block::k_view return today (kv_cache.hpp:2210, 2217), so callers need no conversion.
