cpp-interface, dispo:B13, dispo:B06; 3 of 3 verifiers could not refute it (votes and corrections)

Minor N15. Aligned flag of moved set_v/get_v row views unstated

Where
5. Interface design (One cache row-write rule)
Round-1 link
touches F01, F10
Claim
Ambiguity that makes the implementer guess: the design says the packed append_v_row (the renamed set_v, the V row save) takes 'the existing contiguous row view with the caller's scalar type and alignment', and load_row (the renamed get_v) wraps caller scratch in an existing contiguous view. tron's row view carries an aligned template flag that may be true or false. Today's set_v bodies load the source with aligned instructions (_mm256_load_epi32 for bf16 and fp16, _mm512_load_ps for float) and get_v stores the destination with aligned instructions (_mm512_store_ps, _mm256_store_si256). If the new overloads accept aligned == false views, the implementer must either keep aligned loads (undefined behavior on an unaligned caller) or switch to unaligned loads (an instruction change the design's own section-8 instruction comparison would flag). The design does not say which. Today's callers do satisfy 64-byte alignment: save_v_impl passes &vs[ix][kv_head * head_size] with head_size % 32 == 0 into a chunk-aligned buffer, and the GOF (hardware cache preparation) callback passes caller-owned 64-byte scratch.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
Packed overloads take v_vnni_view<bf16,R,D> or v_vnni_view<const bf16,R,D>. Sources use the existing contiguous row view with the caller's scalar type and alignment. Preserve those properties at the call site. [...] load_row moves today's get_v loops. Support the existing bf16 and float destinations needed by its callers. Wrap caller-owned scratch in an existing contiguous view; add no scratch-view type.
h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp:2300, 2308@2880c3aa9b (bf16x16 lo = _mm256_load_epi32(vout_ptr + i)), :2339, 2349 (fp16 source, _mm256_load_epi32), :2380, 2389 (fp32x16 lo_fp32 = _mm512_load_ps(vout_ptr + i)), :2421, 2430 (get_v float: _mm512_store_ps), :2455, 2463 (get_v bf16: _mm256_store_si256); h/tron/models/model.hpp:2831-2832@2880c3aa9b (pg.template set_v<geometry.kv>(slot, kv_head, j, &vs[ix][kv_head * geometry.kv.head_size])); h/tron/scheduler/full.hpp:2652-2656@2880c3aa9b (v_head_fn passes dest scratch to get_v); h/tron/tensor/view.hpp:116-121@2880c3aa9b (rank-1 view template parameter bool aligned); design.txt:240, :242.
Correction from verification
Ambiguity (kind c), minor: the revised design tells the implementer to move today's set_v and get_v vector bodies unchanged (design.txt:241, :242, :352), which keeps the aligned loads and stores (kv_cache.hpp:2300-2308, 2339-2349, 2380-2389, 2421-2430, 2455-2463@2880c3aa9b), but it never states the value of the aligned template flag of the source and destination row views it passes to append_v_row, load_row, page::set_v and page::get_v (design.txt:240, :252, :354 say only "the caller's ... alignment" and "without changing type or alignment"). Because view.hpp:124-126 performs no alignment check, an overload templated over bool aligned would accept an aligned = false view and run aligned intrinsics on it. Add one sentence: the packed append_v_row and load_row overloads, and the typed page::set_v/get_v wrappers, accept only aligned = true row views (view/const_view<Src, true, dma, seq<D>, sseq<1>>); today's callers satisfy this (model.hpp:2831-2836 via a chunk-aligned tensor buffer, tensor.hpp:34/71, auto.hpp:87; gof.cpp:203/213 via alignas(64) scratch), and the fixture at t/heterogeneous_scheduler_compile.cpp:441 gains alignas(64) to make its existing requirement explicit. Drop the sentence that the section-8 comparison "would flag" an unaligned-load switch: the design's material-change rule (design.txt:442) does not cover a mnemonic swap that changes neither count nor call structure.
Ambiguity (narrowed): design.txt:240 and :354 tell the implementer to keep the caller's alignment at each call site, and today's production sources are 64-byte aligned (model.hpp:2831-2832 into an alignas(chunk_alignment) tensor with head_size % 32 == 0; gof.hpp:62-63 scratch). What the design leaves unstated is (a) whether the append_v_row and load_row overloads accept views with aligned == false at all, and (b) if they do, whether the moved bodies keep their aligned loads/stores (kv_cache.hpp:2300-2308, 2339-2349, 2380-2389, 2421-2463). Recommended one sentence in the row-write rule: "The packed append_v_row and load_row overloads accept only aligned contiguous row views (view/const_view<Src, true, dma, seq<D>, sseq<1>>); the moved bodies keep their aligned instructions. At the model call site build the source with slice<geometry.kv.head_size>()(vs[ix].as_view(), kv_head * geometry.kv.head_size), as the 8-lane branch already does (model.hpp:2834-2836). Add alignas(64) to t/heterogeneous_scheduler_compile.cpp:441, whose std::array<float, 256> source already lacks it today." Drop the claim that section 8's comparison would flag an aligned-to-unaligned swap: its material-change definition (design.txt:442) does not include it, and whether the emitted instructions differ at all is unmeasured.
Keep the finding; correct two sentences. (1) Replace "Today's callers do satisfy 64-byte alignment" with: "Today's production callers satisfy 64-byte alignment (model.hpp:2831-2832 offsets a chunk-aligned btensor by kv_head * head_size with head_size % 32 == 0; full.hpp:2652-2656 passes caller scratch). Most test fixtures use alignas(64) or tensor storage, but t/heterogeneous_scheduler_compile.cpp:441 passes a std::array<float, 256> with no alignas and needs alignas(64) under an aligned = true view." (2) Replace "an instruction change the design's own section-8 instruction comparison would flag" with: "an instruction change that the section-8 record would show but that the design's definition of a material change (design.txt:442) does not cover, so it could pass unremarked." The recommendation to pin aligned = true and keep the moved bodies' aligned loads stands and matches the round-1 F10 recommendation.
Recommendation
Add one sentence to the row-write rule: 'The packed append_v_row and load_row overloads accept only aligned row views (view/const_view<Src, true, dma, seq<D>, sseq<1>>), because the moved bodies keep their aligned loads and stores (kv_cache.hpp:2300-2308, 2380-2389, 2421-2463). Today's callers satisfy this: save_v_impl offsets by kv_head * head_size with head_size % 32 == 0 inside a chunk-aligned buffer (model.hpp:2831-2832), and the GOF callback scratch is 64-byte aligned (S14). Do not switch to unaligned loads.'
Found by
cpp-interface, handoff-actionability; 3 of 3 verifiers could not refute it (votes and corrections)

Minor N16. 8-lane page::set_v: existence ambiguous, wrapper has no caller

Where
5. Interface design (Existing callers and their replacements, row page::v, page::v_ptr)
Round-1 link
touches F10, F11, F11, F01
Claim
Ambiguity: today page::set_v exists only under TRON_CHUNK_SIZE == 16, and save_v_impl (the model function that writes one token's V) has an #if branch: the 16-lane branch calls set_v, the 8-lane branch calls v<geometry>(slot, kv_head, j).set(slice(...)). The revised row says the 8-lane save path 'may forward through the unified typed set_v wrapper'. The implementing agent cannot tell whether the parent adds a page::set_v overload to the 8-lane build and removes the #if in save_v_impl, or leaves both as they are. Section 5's 'Use the same typed kernel declarations in both builds' covers kernels, not this cache method.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
The model's save path may forward through the unified typed set_v wrapper; its conversion remains the old row .set().
kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1616 #if TRON_CHUNK_SIZE == 16 (set_v/get_v), :1674 #else (v(), v_ptr()); model.hpp@2880c3aa9b:2830-2837 #if TRON_CHUNK_SIZE == 16 pg.template set_v<geometry.kv>(...) #else pg.template v<geometry.kv>(slot, kv_head, j).set(slice<geometry.kv.head_size>()(vs[ix].as_view(), ...)) #endif. design.txt:256.
Correction from verification
The finding stands; sharpen it as follows. (a) The design quote is at design.txt:254, not :256. (b) The defect is an internal inconsistency, not only a hedge: design.txt:180 ("The 8-lane forwarding wrapper retains its existing contiguous-row constraints"), :245 (row-major append_v_row overload forwarding to .set()) and :354 ("Keep save_v_impl -> page::set_v -> append_v_row", unqualified by build) presuppose an 8-lane set_v chain that does not exist at main (kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1616-1725 declares set_v only under TRON_CHUNK_SIZE == 16), while :254 says "may" and :312 reads as "unchanged"; the fate of save_v_impl's #if (model.hpp@2880c3aa9b:2830-2837) is never stated. (c) "Wrapper has no caller" holds for production code only: set_v has unguarded test callers (t_llama_unit.cpp@2880c3aa9b:2533/2535/2792/2802 in non-template TEST_CASEs, t_amx_dispatch_dtype.cpp:98, heterogeneous_scheduler_compile.cpp:443), so the 8-lane t_llama_unit and t_heterogeneous_scheduler do not compile at baseline main, and the decision determines whether the design's "8-lane, AMX code off" configuration (design.txt:413) can build the t_llama_unit case required at design.txt:395. The recommendation should therefore also say which of the two routes lets the 8-lane test configuration compile.
Partly right. The revised design is not silent on the 8-lane set_v: three sentences imply that a unified page::set_v exists in both builds. design.txt:180 says "The 8-lane forwarding wrapper retains its existing contiguous-row constraints". design.txt:245 adds a row-major overload of append_v_row (the typed V write operation) that is "a thin forwarder to existing contiguous .set()". design.txt:354 (step 3) says "Keep save_v_impl -> page::set_v -> append_v_row" with no build qualifier. Only design.txt:254 hedges with "may forward through the unified typed set_v wrapper". So the correct claim is: the design implies the decision in three places but hedges it in one, and it never states two consequences an implementer needs: (1) the 8-lane page::set_v is NEW (no set_v exists in the 8-lane page today, so "retains its existing ... constraints" at line 180 is misleading); (2) the #if TRON_CHUNK_SIZE == 16 / #else in save_v_impl (model.hpp@2880c3aa9b:2830-2837) collapses to one set_v call. The R26 sub-claim adds real weight: t/heterogeneous_scheduler_compile.cpp@2880c3aa9b:443 calls page->set_v<software_geometry.kv>(..., values.data()) with a float* source and no TRON_CHUNK_SIZE guard anywhere in the file, and t/CMakeLists.txt@2880c3aa9b:571-574 registers t_heterogeneous_scheduler unconditionally, so the design's own requirement to build that test in the 8-lane configuration (design.txt:400 with :413) can only be met if the parent adds an 8-lane set_v that accepts a float source row. The "leave both as they are" option therefore contradicts design.txt:400; the design should say so instead of "may". Fix: at line 254 replace "may forward" with "forwards"; add "page::set_v is declared in both builds; in the 8-lane build it is new and forwards to the row-major append_v_row overload (.set()); save_v_impl's #if/#else collapses to one set_v call"; repeat the choice in step 3 (line 354). Minor citation slip in the finding: the quoted row is design.txt:254, not :256.
Ambiguity (c), narrowed: the design already decides that an 8-lane page::set_v forwarding wrapper exists (design.txt:180 "The 8-lane forwarding wrapper retains its existing contiguous-row constraints"; :254 "the unified typed set_v wrapper"; :245 row-major append_v_row overload forwards to .set()). What :254's "may" leaves undecided is only whether save_v_impl's #else branch (model.hpp@2880c3aa9b:2833-2837) is redirected to that wrapper and its #if TRON_CHUNK_SIZE == 16 removed. Drop "wrapper has no caller" from the title: t/heterogeneous_scheduler_compile.cpp@2880c3aa9b:443 and t/t_llama_unit.cpp@2880c3aa9b:1723 call set_v with no chunk guard, so the 8-lane wrapper has test callers (and those tests cannot compile at 8 lanes today). Recommendation: replace "may forward" at :254 with "forwards", and add to step 3 (:354) "save_v_impl calls page::set_v in both builds; its #if/#else is removed". If the design instead chooses no 8-lane set_v, it must also revise :180 and the t_heterogeneous_scheduler row at :400 (that test is then 16-lane only).
Recommendation
Decide and state it: either 'The parent adds page::set_v(slot, kv_head, i_page, contiguous row view) to the 8-lane build as a forwarder to v(...).set(); save_v_impl then calls set_v in both builds and its #if is removed', or 'The 8-lane build keeps v(...).set() and the #if in save_v_impl stays'. Name the choice in step 3 of section 7 as well.

Minor N17. copy_plane has no caller; no whole-plane V copy exists

Where
5. Interface design (One cache row-write rule) and 6. Behavior the implementation must preserve (Copies and hardware preparation), 7. step 1
Round-1 link
touches F13
Claim
Design decision I disagree with, and a factual error in the behavior list: the revision adds copy_plane(v_owner& dst, v_owner const& src) and tells the implementer to 'Preserve whole-plane byte copies', but no whole-plane V byte copy exists at main. kv_cache.hpp has three memcpy calls: K rows for count tokens, 8-lane V rows for count tokens, and the EAGLE embedding bytes. The 16-lane V copy goes token by token through append_v. So 'preserve' names a behavior that is not there, and copy_plane is a new operation with no caller. That contradicts the design's own rule to defer operations until a caller needs them (design.txt:63) and the repository rule against speculative code. The round-1 F13 recommendation proposed copy_plane, but two of its three verifier corrections withdrew it because a trivially copyable owner already copies by assignment. The revision followed the withdrawn recommendation.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
copy_plane copies identical-layout owners byte for byte. Cache range splitting and completion copying remain in cache code. [...] Preserve whole-plane byte copies, the four source/destination token-parity combinations, page-boundary splitting, overlap restrictions, and completion-state copying.
kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b memcpy sites: 1941 memcpy(dst, src, count * k_size); (K rows), 1955 memcpy(dst_v, src_v, count * v_size); (8-lane V rows, inside #else of TRON_CHUNK_SIZE == 16), 1981 memcpy(dst, src, count * x_bytes); (EAGLE embeddings); 1944-1946 for (size_t i = 0; i < count; ++i) append_v<geometry>(...) (16-lane V). No other .v copy outside kv_cache.hpp (grep of h/tron, src/tron, t at 2880c3aa9b finds only a comment in self_attention.hpp:1321). r1-findings.md F13 verifier correction 2: 'The recommended copy_plane(owner&, owner const&) is redundant'; verifier correction 3: 'Drop the copy_plane suggestion: owner copy assignment (trivially copyable) already gives the whole-plane byte copy, and at main V never takes that path.' design.txt:236 copy_plane(v_owner& dst, v_owner const& src);, :244, :341, :352; design.txt:63 'Defer slices, iteration, generic expression assignment, and expression constructors until a caller needs them.'
Correction from verification
Keep the finding; adjust two attributions. The copy_plane signature (design.txt:236, :244, :352) is new to the revision, but the sentence "Preserve whole-plane byte copies" (design.txt:341) is inherited from the original design (original text line 438 "Copy a complete plane with a byte copy when source and destination have the same layout") and was not flagged in round 1, so R83's "Revision of F13 introduced a defect" applies only to the signature. Replace "the repository rule against speculative code" with "the design's own scope limit at design.txt:63, 'Limit the parent: expression reads and existing row operations'" (tron AGENTS.md at 2880c3aa9b has no speculative-code rule; that rule is jhan's CLAUDE.md Rule 2). The recommendation stands: remove copy_plane from :236, :244 and :352, and reword :341 to name the actual copies (K rows and 8-lane V rows by memcpy of count tokens at kv_cache.hpp:1941 and :1955; 16-lane V token by token through copy_token at :1943-1946), with one sentence that a whole-plane copy, if ever needed, is the owner's defaulted copy assignment.
Keep the finding, with three adjustments. (1) The revision adds only the named copy_plane signature (design.txt:236, :244, :352). The sentence "Preserve whole-plane byte copies" (design.txt:341) already existed in the original design as "Copy a complete plane with a byte copy when source and destination have the same layout" (quoted in the round-1 F13 verifier correction 2; round-1 page line 1782 says the original section 6 "preserves the whole-plane byte copy"). So the behavior-list error is carried forward, not introduced. (2) The withdrawal of copy_plane appears only in the round-1 votes page (claude-review-design-new-tensor-type-r1-votes.html). The round-1 main page's F13 body (prior-review.txt:727) recommends copy_plane, and its F08 scope table (prior-review.txt:369) lists "whole-plane byte copy" under KEEP. The design therefore followed the round-1 page as shown; it still conflicts with its own rule at design.txt:63. (3) "No whole-plane V byte copy exists at main" is exact for the 16-lane packed path (kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1943-1946, per token). In the 8-lane build, memcpy(dst_v, src_v, count * v_size) at :1955 copies the whole plane when count == page_size, but on the raw array the design keeps as an array (design.txt:322), so a v_owner operation cannot apply there either. The recommendation stands: remove copy_plane from :236, :244 and :352; replace :341 with the actual row-range copies (K rows and 8-lane V rows by memcpy of count tokens, 16-lane V token by token through copy_token); add one sentence that a whole-plane copy, if ever needed, is the owner's defaulted copy assignment.
Keep the finding's core claim and recommendation. Correct three sub-claims. (1) Replace "The revision followed the withdrawn recommendation" with: "The round-1 page codex worked from published the F13 recommendation with copy_plane and listed 'whole-plane byte copy' under KEEP in its scope table (prior-review.txt:707-735, :369); the two verifier corrections that withdrew copy_plane were not shown on that page. Codex followed the published text. The withdrawal is still right: the owner's defaulted copy is the byte copy, and no parent caller needs one." (2) Reclassify "a factual error in the behavior list" as an ambiguity (kind c): "Preserve whole-plane byte copies" (design.txt:341) is carried over from the original design (design-text.txt:438) and the round-1 page accepted it (prior-review.txt:1782). At main, K and 8-lane V are byte-copied as a whole plane only when book::append passes count == page_size through the row-range memcpy (kv_cache.hpp:1039-1046, :1941, :1955 at 2880c3aa9b); no distinct whole-plane path exists, and 16-lane V never byte-copies a plane. The recommended replacement sentence is still the right fix. (3) Replace "the repository rule against speculative code" with a citation of AGENTS.md@2880c3aa9b:118, "Keep changes request-scoped, preserve behavior and APIs, and match local patterns."
Recommendation
Remove copy_plane from the signature block (design.txt:236), the bullet at :244 and step 1 (:352 'plane copy'). Replace 'Preserve whole-plane byte copies' at :341 with 'Preserve the existing row-range copies: K rows and 8-lane V rows by memcpy of count tokens, 16-lane V token by token through copy_token.' Add one sentence: 'A whole-plane copy, if a caller ever needs one, is the owner's defaulted copy assignment; no named operation is added.'
Lead's reading
The revision followed the round-1 F13 recommendation literally, although two of the three round-1 verifiers had withdrawn the copy_plane suggestion in their corrections. That is a fair criticism of the round-1 page as much as of the revision: the recommendation text should have carried the correction. The fix is to drop copy_plane or name a caller.
Found by
behavior-arena, simplicity-scope, handoff-actionability, dispo:B01; 3 of 3 verifiers could not refute it (votes and corrections)

Minor N18. page::v_tensor returns a view, contradicting owner/view vocabulary

Where
5. Interface design (Existing callers and their replacements)
Round-1 link
touches F10, F39, F39
Claim
Ambiguity in naming: the replacement accessor is named v_tensor but returns a view, while the Words table reserves 'tensor' for the owner (v_vnni_tensor) and 'view' for non-owning access, and the child's equivalent accessor is named k_vnni_plane and returns a view. The row also does not state the 8-lane return type.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
Remove after all callers migrate. Add page::v_tensor<geometry>(slot, head).
design.txt:43 'v_vnni_tensor owns packed V. v_vnni_view accesses a packed V matrix.'; design.txt:31 (owner and view); design.txt:255; design.txt:377 'page::k_vnni_plane ... Its replacement returns k_vnni_view<const bf16,64,128>'; h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:2208-2212 kv_block::k_view() returns view<bf16, true, false, seq<page_size, head_size>> (the natural 8-lane V analogue).
Correction from verification
Finding stands as written, with two additions: (a) say that round-1 F39 recommended the name page::v_tensor and this finding reverses that recommendation; (b) the recommendation should offer "declare v_tensor only in the 16-lane build" as the alternative to stating an 8-lane return type, since the sole caller of v_data (self_attention.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1681) is under #ifdef TRON_AMX_DISPATCH and no 8-lane AMX-on build is supported (design.txt:413).
Ambiguity (naming) and omission, both minor: the design's own Words table makes "tensor" the owner (v_vnni_tensor owns; v_vnni_view accesses) [design.txt:26, :31, :43], yet the new page accessor is named page::v_tensor and returns a view [design.txt:255]; Ben's sketch names page accessors by layout (k_packed, v_packed) and returns as_view() [comment 5765866077 s.5]; tron names view-returning accessors k, k_view, v, never *_tensor [kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1548-1571, 1676-1719, 2208-2220]. Note that the v_tensor name was the round-1 review's own F39 recommendation, so this is a refinement of prior guidance, not a codex regression [prior-review.txt:1199]. The row also leaves the 8-lane return type unstated; that overload has no caller in the parent because v_data's only caller (self_attention.hpp:1681) is compiled only with TRON_AMX_DISPATCH, which self_attention.hpp:30-36 forbids at TRON_CHUNK_SIZE != 16. Recommended fix: rename to page::v_view<geometry>(slot, kv_head) (or v_plane), state that it returns the owner's as_view(), and state both return types: 16-lane v_vnni_view<bf16, page_size, geometry.head_size> (const page: v_vnni_view<const bf16,...>); 8-lane view<bf16,true,false,seq<page_size, geometry.head_size>> (const page: const_view), the form kv_block::k_view uses, or say the accessor is 16-lane only. Precision: at child head 30c4ac82cb, k_vnni_plane returns const bf16* [kv_cache.hpp:1689]; the design says its replacement returns k_vnni_view but does not name the replacement [design.txt:377], so the K side needs the same naming decision.
Note (no change required; a rename is optional): the accessor page::v_tensor<geometry>(slot, head) returns a view, while the design's own vocabulary reserves "tensor" for the owner (design.txt:26 "tensor stores values in host memory"; :43 "v_vnni_tensor owns packed V. v_vnni_view accesses a packed V matrix"; :31 owner vs view). The name page::v_tensor was recommended verbatim by round-1 finding F39 ("Add only page::v_tensor (page::v already exists in the 8-lane build with a row signature, so keep the distinct name)"), and the design lists F39 as Accepted (design.txt:463). If the panel wants a rename, it must say it is reversing F39's name recommendation. tron precedent for view-returning accessors is page::k (kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1547-1570) and kv_block::k_view() (:2207-2212); no tron accessor named *_tensor() returns a view. So page::v_view or page::v_plane would match tron precedent better. "v_packed" (R49's alternative) is unsuitable: the accessor is build-selected and returns a row-major view in the 8-lane build (design.txt:255), so a layout name would be wrong in that build. The design does not say the child's replacement accessor keeps the name k_vnni_plane; design.txt:377 says only "Remove the raw-pointer accessor. Its replacement returns k_vnni_view<const bf16,64,128>", so the parent/child naming mismatch the finding asserts is not fixed by the design text. Optional clarification: state the 8-lane return type, view<bf16, true, false, seq<page_size, geometry.head_size>> mirroring kv_block::k_view (const_view for const pages), and v_vnni_view<bf16 or const bf16, page_size, geometry.head_size> for the 16-lane build. Consequence is low: the contract column already says "Return the packed or row-major view selected by the build" (design.txt:255), so an implementing agent builds a view-returning accessor whatever the name, and the only production caller today (self_attention.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1681) is inside #ifdef TRON_AMX_DISPATCH (1571-1701), so the 8-lane plane view has no production caller.
Recommendation
Rename to page::v_view<geometry>(slot, kv_head) (or v_plane) and state both return types: 16-lane v_vnni_view<bf16, page_size, geometry.head_size> (const page: const bf16), 8-lane view<bf16, true, false, seq<page_size, geometry.head_size>> like kv_block::k_view.
Found by
handoff-actionability, ben-fidelity; 3 of 3 verifiers could not refute it (votes and corrections)

Minor N20. v_vnni_row constructor not covered by no-public-pointer rule

Where
5. Interface design (operation contract table)
Claim
Ambiguity. The reviewer's rule is that a private constructor prevents relabelling an arbitrary row-major pointer as packed data. The design applies "No public pointer constructor ... for packed views", but v_vnni_row (the logical-row expression type) "stores its pair address and even/odd position by value" and is not called a view, so an implementing agent may give it a public (pointer, parity) constructor; any caller could then relabel a pointer as a packed row and write through it.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
View copy / conversion Copying a view rebinds access. Allow writable-to-read-only conversion only. No public pointer constructor, .data member, or pointer conversion for packed views.
Ben comment 5765866077: "The private constructor prevents routine callers from relabelling an arbitrary row-major pointer as packed data." design.txt:212, :222; design.txt:43 (Words: "v_vnni_row reads one logical row").
Correction from verification
The finding stands; sharpen its recommendation in two places. (1) The friend list needs one more entry: the design requires writable-to-read-only conversion for all packed types [design.txt:222], so v_vnni_row<const bf16,D> must accept v_vnni_row<bf16,D>; give the row a template <typename, size_t> friend class v_vnni_row; declaration (Ben's sketch does the same for vnni_view with template <typename, typename> friend class vnni_view;). (2) Replace "detail::vnni_access is the only pointer reader" with "the row exposes no pointer; its own operator[], chunk() and bf16_chunk() are the only readers of the stored pair address, and detail::vnni_access need not be its friend", because the design's bulk operations take matrix views plus a token index, not rows [design.txt:231-236]. Optionally add "reject a v_vnni_row constructed from a raw pointer" to the compile-check row at design.txt:397, so the rule has a test.
Claim stands as written. Sharpen the recommendation in two places: (1) also befriend v_vnni_row<bf16,D> from v_vnni_row<const bf16,D> so the writable-to-read-only conversion the design allows at design.txt:222 still compiles with a private constructor; (2) add "construct a packed row from a raw pointer" to the reject list of the compile-check row at design.txt:397, so the rule is tested. Note for the lead: the kernel boundary is unaffected either way, because kernels take v_vnni_view and no row-to-view conversion exists.
Recommendation
Add to the contract row: "v_vnni_row has the same restrictions: its (pair address, parity) constructor is private, v_vnni_view<T,R,D> is its friend and only producer, it exposes no pointer, and detail::vnni_access is the only pointer reader."
Found by
ben-fidelity; 3 of 3 verifiers could not refute it (votes and corrections)

Minor N23. page::v_tensor 8-lane row-major branch has no caller

Where
5. Interface design, accessor table (page::v_data row)
Round-1 link
touches F39, F10
Claim
Design decision I disagree with, plus an ambiguity: the only external reader of a whole V plane is the AMX PV call in self_attention.hpp, and AMX dispatch is a compile error in the 8-lane build. page::scaled_v is a page member and reaches kv_block directly. No rank-2 row-major V view exists in the 8-lane build today, so the "row-major view selected by the build" is a new accessor type with zero callers. The row also does not spell the return type, and the name v_tensor conflicts with the design's own vocabulary (tensor = owner, view = access), so an agent could return the owner by reference.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
page::v_data, kv_block::v_base | Remove after all callers migrate. Add page::v_tensor<geometry>(slot, head). | Return the packed or row-major view selected by the build. Const pages return read-only views.
h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp:1680-1682@2880c3aa9b (only v_data caller, inside the AMX page path); :30-35 (#error for AMX with TRON_CHUNK_SIZE != 16); h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp:1730-1742 (scaled_v is a page member); :1676-1724 (8-lane v()/v_ptr() return rank-1 rows only). (b) Replace "a new accessor type with zero callers" with: "a new page accessor whose 8-lane branch returns a rank-2 row-major V view that nothing calls; the view type itself already exists (kv_block::k_view returns the same template at kv_cache.hpp:2207-2211)." Add the context that today's page::v_data / kv_block::v_base are also compiled unconditionally with no 8-lane caller (kv_cache.hpp:1606-1613, 2233-2240 lie outside the TRON_CHUNK_SIZE == 16 blocks), so the parent can drop that dead branch rather than re-create it under a new name.
Keep the finding with two adjustments. (1) Replace "a new accessor type with zero callers" with "a new accessor branch, returning an already-existing row-major view type (const_view<bf16,true,false,seq<page_size,head_size>>, the shape kv_block::k_view() already uses at kv_cache.hpp:2208-2220), with zero callers in the design text and in main"; the type is not new, the callerless branch and its reinterpret_cast construction over the raw 8-lane array are. (2) Soften "an agent could return the owner by reference": the row at design.txt line 255 says "Return the packed or row-major view", so the return kind is stated; the residual defect is only the name inconsistency with line 43 ("v_vnni_tensor owns packed V. v_vnni_view accesses a packed V matrix"), and the panel should note that round-1 F39's own recommendation proposed the name page::v_tensor, which the design adopted. Corrected claim: "page::v_tensor<geometry>(slot, head) (design.txt line 255) is specified with a build-selected 8-lane row-major branch that no caller in main or in the design uses (the only whole-plane reader is the AMX PV call at self_attention.hpp:1681 inside #ifdef TRON_AMX_DISPATCH, a #error under TRON_CHUNK_SIZE != 16 at :30-36; page::scaled_v reaches kv_block directly at kv_cache.hpp:2252-2262), the row spells no return type for either branch, and the name says tensor while the design's vocabulary reserves tensor for the owner." Two narrowings, neither changes the verdict. (1) The sub-claim "an agent could return the owner by reference" is overstated: the row at design.txt line 255 says "Return the packed or row-major view", and section 6 forbids casting storage to the owner, so the naming inconsistency (v_tensor for a view) is a glossary conflict, not a realistic wrong-build risk. (2) The name page::v_tensor came from the round-1 review's own recommendations (F39: "Add only page::v_tensor"; F08: "page::v_tensor (or v_plane) const view"), so the finding should credit that origin rather than present the name as the design's choice. Minor N24. 8-lane scaled_v_expr constructor input unaddressed

Where
revision of F42
Round-1 link
touches F42
Claim
Kind (c) ambiguity introduced by narrowed wording. Claim: design.txt:257 now says "Give the expression constructor a typed packed V view", but the 8-lane build stores V row-major, and its scaled_v_expr constructor today also takes a raw const bf16s* data [kv_cache.hpp:2139-2143@f46e48bab498]; the design does not say what typed argument the 8-lane constructor takes. The original design said "a typed V input" (build-neutral). An implementer may leave the 8-lane constructor on a raw pointer, which contradicts design.txt:316 "Obtain the physical address only inside the typed implementation". Evidence: design.txt:257 versus design.txt:255 ("Return the packed or row-major view selected by the build") and design.txt:316. Recommendation: reword design.txt:257 to "Give the expression constructor the typed V view of the build: v_vnni_view<const bf16, 64, D> in the 16-lane build, the row-major const view in the 8-lane build (the type page::v_tensor returns)."
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
design.txt:257: "page::scaled_v, detail::scaled_v_expr | Keep the page method. Give the expression constructor a typed packed V view. | Keep scales, live-token count, and scratch as separate arguments. The 16-lane packed bound is (count + 1) / 2 * 2; the 8-lane row-major bound is count. Neither uses the view's static row count." design.txt:316: "scaled_v_expr | Keep the live count separate from the storage shape. Keep pair rounding only in the 16-lane packed path; the 8-lane row-major path reads exactly count rows. Preserve supported widths, weight conversion, unrolled loops, and addition order. Obtain the physical address only inside the typed implementation." design.txt:283: "A packed-V weighted sum rounds an odd live-token count up to a complete pair. ... The row-major path reads exactly the live-token count." design.txt:353 (step 2): "Keep typed scaled_v_expr here unless a real include dependency requires a separate header." design.txt:463: "Live count stays separate from shape."
Correction from verification
The claim stands as filed. Two corrections to the finding record: (a) The JSON "recommendation" field ("append 'count comes from page::count(), as today', and define 'live-token count'") does not match the claim's own recommendation (reword design.txt:257 to name the 8-lane type). The claim-text recommendation is the one that resolves this finding. The "live-token count" wording point is true (Words used here, design.txt:15-45, defines only "Valid token row"; the design uses "live-token count" at 257 and 283 and "live count" at 316, 395, 463) but it is a separate minor wording nit, not this finding. (b) The proposed rewording "(the type page::v_tensor returns)" is itself unresolved: the design never names the 8-lane v_tensor return type (design.txt:255 says only "row-major view selected by the build", section 5 declares no row-major V view). The rewording of design.txt:257 should name it, for example: "In the 16-lane build the constructor takes v_vnni_view<const bf16, PAGE_TOKENS_64, D>; in the 8-lane build it takes the rank-2 row-major const_view<bf16, true, false, seq<PAGE_TOKENS_64, D>> over the plane, the same type page::v_tensor returns in that build. Neither build keeps the const bf16s* data constructor."
Narrowed claim: design.txt:257 "Give the expression constructor a typed packed V view" is wrong for the 8-lane build, where V stays row-major [design.txt:270, 322]. The design already implies both builds pass a view [design.txt:257 "Neither uses the view's static row count"; :316 "Obtain the physical address only inside the typed implementation"; :255 v_tensor returns "the packed or row-major view selected by the build"], so the likely wrong reading is not "leave the 8-lane constructor on a raw pointer" but "hand the 8-lane constructor the packed view type, which compiles under TRON_CHUNK_SIZE == 8 [design.txt:270] but has no packed data behind it". Fix: reword 257 to "Give the expression constructor the typed read-only V view of the build, the type page::v_tensor returns: v_vnni_view<const bf16, 64, D> in the 16-lane build, the existing contiguous row-major const view in the 8-lane build." Also add "live-token count (page::count(), the number of valid token rows)" to Words used here, or replace "live-token count"/"live count" at 257, 283, 316, 395, 463 with the defined term "valid token" [design.txt:45; kv_cache.hpp:2261@f46e48bab498].
Two attributions in the finding need adjusting; neither weakens it. (a) The packed-only wording was not invented by codex's revision. Round-1 F10's recommendation said "page::scaled_v (keep; scaled_v_expr constructor takes vnni_view<const bf16, page_size, head_size>)" [r1-findings.md:68], which is itself 16-lane only; the revised design adopted it without adding the 8-lane case. (b) "live-token count" is the tron source's own word: kv_cache.hpp@f46e48bab498:1483-1494 documents page::count() as "How many kv entries are live". So the terminology item is a define-at-first-use gap (add "live-token count = page::count(), the number of live tokens on the page" to Words used here or to design.txt:257), not a wrong term. Corrected claim: design.txt:257 tells the implementer to give scaled_v_expr a typed packed V view, but the 8-lane build keeps row-major V (design.txt:270, 322; kv_cache.hpp:2199-2200@f46e48bab498) and its constructor today takes const bf16s* data (kv_cache.hpp:2139-2142); the design names no typed input for that path and never names page::count() as the count source, so the implementer must guess between a raw pointer and the row-major const view. Fix: reword design.txt:257 to "Give the expression constructor the typed V view of the build: v_vnni_view<const bf16, 64, D> in the 16-lane build, the row-major const_view (the type page::v_tensor returns) in the 8-lane build. count comes from page::count(), as today."
Recommendation
Optional: in design.txt:257 append "count comes from page::count(), as today", and either add "live-token count" to Words used here or reuse the defined term "valid token".

Minor N25. EAGLE bullet omits uniform-geometry requirement; misread as heterogeneous

Where
6. Behavior the implementation must preserve (Arena construction without clearing)
Round-1 link
touches C01, C09, C01
Claim
Ambiguity and omission: the EAGLE bullet follows the heterogeneous-geometry bullet and reads as part of that enumeration. EAGLE (the speculative model's extra cache slot) exists only with uniform geometry: eagle_compatible requires layout_t::uniform_geometry, storage_slot_count_for_layers asserts it, and calculate_allocation_sizes returns nullopt otherwise. Under uniform geometry the design's own rule is one array over the whole arena, which already covers the extra slot because arena_sizes adds it to the retained arena. So the heterogeneous enumeration never meets an EAGLE slot, and the bullet's warning about 'the logical active-slot loop' applies to a loop the design does not prescribe for that case. An implementing agent may add a dead EAGLE branch to the heterogeneous path or search for a heterogeneous EAGLE slot that cannot exist. Round-1 C01 asked for exactly this statement and the revision did not add it.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
Include the physical EAGLE KV slot once, with the primary geometry. Do not touch x_data, the separate EAGLE embedding buffer; it contains no KV blocks. Construction cannot rely solely on the logical active-slot loop, which omits that extra physical slot.
kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:771 eagle_compatible = layout_t::uniform_geometry && x_bytes > 0; :788-789 TRON_ASSERT "EAGLE cache views require uniform geometry and embedding storage"; :1158 if (support_eagle && (!eagle_compatible || ...)) return std::nullopt;; :717-719 arena_sizes adds offsets.values[1].total() to result.retained; :1288-1289 uniform accessor indexes one array from kv_blocks_alias. design.txt:331-333. r1-findings.md C01 claim: 'Because EAGLE requires uniform geometry, a single array construction over the whole retained arena already covers the extra slot; the design does not say this either.'
Correction from verification
One refinement, not a refutation. The finding says the bullet's caution about "the logical active-slot loop" applies to a loop the design does not prescribe. The caution is still correct and useful: for_each_active_slot (kv_cache.hpp:1243-1249) iterates only slot < logical_slot_count() over slot_specs and cannot reach the EAGLE offset, and fill_random (:1060-1064) needs a separate fill_storage_slot<layout_t::primary_geometry>(eagle_storage_offset(), ...) call; round-1 C09's verifier correction named exactly that for_each_active_slot + EAGLE pattern as the precedent to reuse. N25's recommended rewrite drops the caution; keep it, qualified as in R09 ("if the implementer uses a per-offset loop for uniform layouts too, it must run over offsets 0 .. storage_slot_count_ - 1 and include eagle_storage_offset() with layout_t::primary_geometry; for_each_active_slot omits it"). Add to the rewrite that the EAGLE slot is the last primary-geometry slot of the RETAINED arena (:727-735, :716-719) and is constructed only in the constructor because restore re-allocates only the reclaimable arena (:968); without the arena placement the reader cannot link line 332's retained-arena sentence to EAGLE.
Two precisions to the finding's evidence, neither changes the verdict. (1) The design's heterogeneous bullet prescribes enumerating PHYSICAL storage offsets (design.txt:332: "enumerate physical storage offsets once"), so the sentence "Construction cannot rely solely on the logical active-slot loop" (design.txt:333) is a caution against the existing for_each_active_slot idiom [kv_cache.hpp@2880c3aa9b:1059-1064, where fill_random adds a separate eagle_storage_offset() call], which the design never prescribes; the finding should say the sentence warns against an approach the design does not use, rather than that it "applies to a loop the design does not prescribe for that case". (2) The cited "uniform accessor indexes one array from kv_blocks_alias" [kv_cache.hpp:1288-1289] is the uniform_reclaimability branch only; the else branch [1291-1299] uses slot_base per physical slot. This does not weaken the argument: the EAGLE slot is in the retained arena in both cases [storage_location :731-734], so the per-arena array of design.txt:331 covers it. Add to the recommendation, from R09 and round-1 C01 (b): "The EAGLE slot is constructed only in the constructor, because restore_reclaimable_kv_storage re-allocates the reclaimable arena only [kv_cache.hpp:968]."
Recommendation
Rewrite the bullet: 'EAGLE storage exists only with uniform geometry [kv_cache.hpp:771, 788-789, 1158]. The whole-arena array of the uniform case therefore already covers the extra EAGLE KV slot, which arena_sizes appends to the retained arena [717-719]. The heterogeneous enumeration never meets an EAGLE slot. Do not touch x_data, the separate EAGLE embedding buffer; it contains no KV blocks.' Move the bullet directly after the uniform-geometry bullet.
Found by
formulas-and-page, behavior-arena, handoff-actionability, dispo:B08; 3 of 3 verifiers could not refute it (votes and corrections)

Minor N31. K token lane sentence imprecise: one lane per line, 64 lines

Where
7. Implementation steps and files (K stores differ from V stores)
Round-1 link
touches C05
Claim
Factual imprecision: the sentence says a K token owns several 4-byte lanes in one shared cache line. In the child layout a token owns exactly one 4-byte lane (its two consecutive dimensions) in each of 64 different 64-byte lines, and 16 tokens share every line. The rest of the paragraph is right: the row store is 4 dword scatters, the block store uses lane-masked stores, and the shared save lets two threads write disjoint lanes of the same lines.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
A K token owns separate 4-byte lanes in a shared cache line.
h/tron/kernels/k_vnni.hpp@30c4ac82cb:16-17 "One 64-byte row holds that dimension pair for 16 consecutive tokens"; :29-30 "Token i owns column (i % 16) of token block (i / 16) in every panel"; :139-146 scatter_row = 4 x _mm512_i32scatter_epi32 with DWORD_BYTES_4 scale; :210-214 masked _mm512_mask_storeu_epi32(panel + dp * ROW_ELEMS_32, present, r[dp]). h/tron/gof.hpp@30c4ac82cb (child diff) "One 64-byte line holds that 4-byte unit for 16 tokens, so a token's 128 values sit in 64 lines". h/tron/models/common.hpp@30c4ac82cb (child diff k_store_window_t comment) "Their writers touch disjoint token lanes (masked stores). So two threads may write one block without conflict."
Correction from verification
The finding stands as written. Two additions: (1) the design's index formula at design.txt:176-178 already encodes the correct layout (one 4-byte lane per token per line, 64 lines per 128-dimension token), so the fix is confined to the one sentence at design.txt:383 and the recommended replacement is consistent with the rest of the design; (2) R93's remark that the [S10] link "resolves the reading" is only partly true, because the link lands at k_vnni.hpp#L42-L310 at 30c4ac82cbb6, after the layout comment at lines 1-31 that carries the "Token i owns column (i % 16)" sentence. Recommended sentence: "A K token owns one 4-byte lane (two consecutive dimensions) in each of the 64 cache lines of its token block. Sixteen tokens share every line."

Ambiguity (child scope), narrowed to set_k_row and get_k_row only: the child table row [design.txt:378] does not state the type of the row parameter (the one-token source or destination buffer). Recommendation

Minor N35. Step 4 mandates K row expression no child caller reads

Where
7. Implementation steps and files (child step 4)
Round-1 link
touches F08
Claim
Design decision I disagree with (child scope): step 4 requires a K row expression, but no reader in the child indexes a VNNI-K row through expr. The dense path passes the whole plane to the AMX kernel, the partial path passes the whole plane to k_vnni::qk_group, the FPGA staging path uses get_k_row into scratch, and page copies use gather_row plus scatter_row. The parent's own rule defers expression features until a caller needs them. A K row chunk would be a strided dword gather across 8 lines, so the requirement adds cost without a consumer.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
Define k_vnni_tensor, k_vnni_view, and its row expression in h/tron/tensor/k_vnni.hpp.
h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp@30c4ac82cb:1437-1439 qk_vnni_128x4(pg.template k_vnni_plane<geometry.kv>(slot, kv_head), q_packed, ...); :1635-1639 k_vnni::qk_group<operation_kv_mul>(page.template k_vnni_plane<geometry.kv>(slot, kv_head), q.data + ..., live, ...); child diff "[[maybe_unused]] auto keys ... return 0; // no row view: the VNNI plane has no contiguous rows". h/tron/scheduler/full.hpp@30c4ac82cb:2644-2655 pg_ptr->template get_k_row<hw_kv>(slot, kv_head, off, dest). kv_cache.hpp child diff copy_storage_slot: k_vnni::gather_row(...); k_vnni::scatter_row(...). design.txt:63 "Defer slices, iteration, generic expression assignment, and expression constructors until a caller needs them."
Correction from verification
Step 4 (design.txt:369) requires a K row expression that no child reader uses through expr; every VNNI-K reader at 30c4ac82cbb6 takes the whole plane (self_attention.hpp:1437-1439, :1636-1639), a gathered scratch row (full.hpp:2655), or gather_row plus scatter_row (kv_cache.hpp:2111-2113). The requirement is not against the design's scope rule: design.txt:63 keeps expression reads and defers only slices, iteration, generic assignment, and expression constructors, and design.txt:56 derives "rank-2 expression access and logical row reads" from Ben's #2, which was addressed to the K VNNI type. What is missing is a stated purpose and consumer: the child section should say the K row expression exists for expr compatibility (uniform tensor interface per Ben #2, host-tensor construction as in the parent's test row at design.txt:398) and add a child test consumer, or else defer it with one sentence and keep load_row (the 4-gather path) as the typed row read. It should also state that a K row chunk is an 8-dword gather across 8 lines (k_vnni.hpp:110-111), so expr reads of K are not a hot-path interface.
Corrected claim: Step 4 of the child plan [design.txt:369] requires k_vnni_view to have a row expression, but no reader at PR 4424 head 30c4ac82cbb6 indexes a VNNI-K row through expr (dense path: self_attention.hpp:1437-1440 whole plane; partial path: :1636-1639 whole plane, :1597-1603 placeholder "no row view"; FPGA staging: full.hpp:2648-2656 get_k_row; copies: kv_cache.hpp:2099-2115 gather_row/scatter_row; tests: set_k_row/get_k_row and raw planes). The design's own child accessor table [design.txt:375-381] maps every K reader to typed bulk operations or typed kernel arguments, and its "Child only" verification row [design.txt:404] tests no K expression read, so the mandated feature has neither a named consumer nor a test. The design's scoping rule [design.txt:63] keeps expression reads and defers only slices, iteration, generic assignment and expression constructors; the finding should not cite it as deferring expression features. The design's expr choice comes from Ben's review #2 ("uniform interface based upon expr"), while Ben's straw-man #3 has at() only and no operator[]; the design should record which of the two it applies to the child's K type. Recommendation stands: either name the consumer of a K row expression (and add a Child-only verification row for it) or state that k_vnni_view is not an expr in the child (at() plus typed store_row/load_row/store_block and the typed plane argument, as in Ben's #3), keeping load_row (the gather) as the typed row read. A 16-dimension chunk read would be an 8-dword gather across 8 lines 64 bytes apart [k_vnni.hpp@30c4ac82cbb6:101-124]; the cost at issue is implementation and test effort, not runtime.
Design decision, partly right (child scope). Facts: no child code reads a VNNI-K row through expr (dense and partial QK take the whole plane [self_attention.hpp@30c4ac82cb:1437-1439, 1635-1639]; the software loop states "no row view" for VNNI slots [:1594-1603]; staging uses get_k_row [full.hpp@30c4ac82cb:2655]; copies use gather_row + scatter_row [kv_cache.hpp@30c4ac82cb:2111]; all tests use set_k_row/get_k_row), and a K row chunk would be an 8-line dword gather. Corrections to the finding's argument: design.txt:63 keeps expression reads and defers only slices, iteration, generic assignment and expression constructors, so it does not support deferring a K row expression; the design mandates the K row expression for uniformity with V under Ben's comment #2 ("uniform interface based upon expr"), recorded as a decision at design.txt:56 and as an open question to Ben at design.txt:84; V's row expression likewise has no production caller and was kept by round-1 F08 as the cheap "usual tensor operation"; round-1 C05 recommended a k_vnni_row with expr<row, D>. Corrected recommendation: in child item 4 (design.txt:369), tie the K row expression to Ben's answer on the operator[]/expr question (design.txt:84). If Ben accepts expr access, keep it and add one sentence: k_vnni_row::chunk() is an inspection/test read (one dword gather across 8 lines per 16 dimensions, built from the gather_row pattern), and hot paths (staging, page copies, QK) use load_row, store_row, store_block and the whole-plane kernel arguments, never the row expression. If Ben declines expr access, drop the K row expression together with V's. Either way, state the choice; do not leave "its row expression" as a bare requirement.
Recommendation
Either name the caller that reads a VNNI-K row through expr, or move "K row expression" to the deferred list for the child and keep load_row (the gather) as the typed row read.

child-needs; 3 of 3 verifiers could not refute it (votes and corrections)

Minor N36. No method for 'collected and executed' evidence; lane can skip tests

Where
7. Implementation steps and files (step 7) and 8. Verification and acceptance (CI ownership; Definition of done)
Round-1 link
touches F16
Claim
Omission and ambiguity: the design requires the implementer to record that the required suite collected and executed the new cases, and AGENTS.md makes that verification mandatory before committing a test, but the design names no place to look and does not warn that the required GCP Nix lane can report "Test host" green without running any test. The lane sets RUN_TESTS false when the exact test-runtime identity already has an NFS passage marker, and only a GitHub rerun forces execution. An implementer who reads a green check as execution evidence records a false claim.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
Run the applicable commands in section 8. Record the required suite's collected and executed cases. Preserve the separately tracked AMX CI gap.

AGENTS.md@2880c3aa9b:22-24: "Before committing it, verify that a required CI suite collects and executes it; local execution and the test file's presence are not sufficient." .github/workflows/gcp-nix.yml@2880c3aa9b:538 RUN_TESTS: ${{ needs.tron-build.outputs.tests_required == 'true' || github.run_attempt > 1 }}, :544-590 every test step if: env.RUN_TESTS == 'true', :675-698 "Mark exact test identity passed" / "Verify cached test identity" via bin/ci/tron-build/test_runtime.py. README.ci.md@2880c3aa9b:586-588: "An empty NFS passage marker records that one exact test-runtime and test-environment pair passed both executor jobs. Correction from verification
Omission/ambiguity (kind c/d), minor: design step 7 (design.txt:358) and the CI paragraph (:423, :452) require the implementer to record required-suite collection and execution evidence but name no artifact, and the run-tron-tests skill they point to (:429) covers only local logs and make list-tests. Drop the sentence "An implementer who reads a green check as execution evidence records a false claim": the GCP Nix passage marker (test_runtime.py@2880c3aa9b:30-51) is written only after both executor jobs ran and passed for the identical source hash and build closure (gcp-nix.yml@2880c3aa9b:671-686, require_results.py:10-20), so a reused-marker green Test host has execution evidence in an earlier run; the risk is citing the wrong run's log, not asserting something untrue. Reduced recommendation: add one sentence to step 7 or the CI paragraph naming the artifact, for example "Evidence is the GCP Nix Test host job log of a run whose test steps ran (RUN_TESTS true: first attempt on a new tree, or a GitHub rerun); a run that reused a passage marker (README.ci.md 'Result Reuse') shows skipped steps, so cite the earlier run that executed the identical closure. Record the run URL and attempt number." Note that the parent's own code commits always change the identity, so attempt 1 on each new head executes.
One precision to add to the finding, not a refutation. The passage marker is keyed by three things, not only the test-runtime identity: a source key (first 7 hex characters of sha256 over git ls-files -s, the tracked-file index, excluding compile_commands.json) [gcp-nix.yml@2880c3aa9b:486-489], plus the test-runtime and test-environment store paths [test_runtime.py@2880c3aa9b:30-36, 45-51: root / source_key / f"nix-tests-{identity}.passed"]. Any change to a tracked file therefore produces a new marker path, so the first CI attempt after a real code or test change executes the tests. Reuse happens when an identical tracked tree is run again within the three-day marker lifetime [README.ci.md@2880c3aa9b:509]: a commit-message-only amend, a rebase that changes no file content, or a re-push of the same tree. The finding's recommendation should say this, and should add the visible sign of reuse: in the "Test host" job every step after checkout shows as skipped, and the final "Test" job runs the step "Verify cached test identity" instead of "Mark exact test identity passed" [gcp-nix.yml@2880c3aa9b:675-698].
Omission and ambiguity: the design requires the implementer to record that the required suite collected and executed the new cases [design.txt:358, 411, 423, 452] and AGENTS.md@2880c3aa9b:22-24 makes that verification mandatory before committing, but the design names no CI-side source for that evidence and does not warn that the required GCP Nix "Test host" job reports success with every test step skipped when the exact source hash plus test-runtime identity already has an NFS passage marker [gcp-nix.yml@2880c3aa9b:538, 541-590, 676-696; test_runtime.py@2880c3aa9b:45-51, 70-74; README.ci.md@2880c3aa9b:586-588]. The run-tron-tests skill the design cites [design.txt:429] gives only the local Slice log location. Because the marker key includes the source hash of the whole tracked tree [gcp-nix.yml:489], the first attempt of any newly pushed commit executes the tests; the skip applies to a merge-queue run of an unchanged tree or a re-push of an identical tree. The recommendation stands: name the Test host job log of a run whose test steps ran (RUN_TESTS true) as the evidence, record run URL and attempt number, and state that a green Test host with skipped steps is not execution evidence.
Recommendation
Add to step 7 or the CI paragraph: "Evidence of execution is the GCP Nix Test host job log of a run whose test steps ran (RUN_TESTS true: first attempt with a changed test runtime, or a GitHub rerun), showing Slice executing t_llama_unit and t_heterogeneous_scheduler with the new case names. A green Test host whose steps were skipped by a cached passage marker (README.ci.md 'Result Reuse') is not execution evidence. Record the run URL and attempt number."

Minor N39. -DAVX512=ON silently overridden on host without AVX-512

Where
8. Verification and acceptance (Build configurations, Commands and execution hosts)
Round-1 link
touches F47
Claim
Omission: the 16-lane rows pass -DAVX512=ON to preset native, but that preset keeps BUILD_NATIVE on, and CMake probes lscpu and sets AVX512 OFF with only a warning when any required feature is missing, so on a build host without AVX-512 both '16-lane' rows silently become 8-lane builds. The design names sw-dev-01 as the build host without stating whether it has AVX-512, and does not name the cross-avx512 preset that exists for this case.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
cmake --preset native -DAVX512=ON -DTRON_AMX_DISPATCH=OFF
CMakeLists.txt@2880c3aa9b:38 'option(BUILD_NATIVE "Probe this host before enabling AVX512" ON)'; :95-116 'if (BUILD_NATIVE) ... foreach(FEATURE ${REQUIRED_AVX512_FEATURES}) if(NOT ...) set(AVX512 OFF) message(WARNING "Disabling AVX512: ${FEATURE} not supported")'; CMakePresets.json@2880c3aa9b:41-49 preset cross-avx512 'Cross-compile for AVX-512 from a non-AVX-512 host' with BUILD_NATIVE OFF, AVX512 ON; design.txt:411-412, 430.
Correction from verification
The omission holds, but the mechanism sentence over-reaches. Only the "16-lane, AMX code off" row (design.txt:411) can silently become an 8-lane build. The "16-lane, AMX code on" row (design.txt:412) cannot degrade silently: the TRON_AMX_DISPATCH definition does not depend on AVX512 (src/tron/CMakeLists.txt@2880c3aa9b:219-224, 329-332), and h/tron/models/self_attention.hpp@2880c3aa9b:30-36 has #if defined(TRON_AMX_DISPATCH) && TRON_CHUNK_SIZE != 16 followed by #error "TRON_AMX_DISPATCH requires TRON_CHUNK_SIZE == 16 ...", so that row fails at compile time on a host where the probe turns AVX512 off. Corrected claim: "Omission: the 16-lane rows pass -DAVX512=ON to preset native, which keeps BUILD_NATIVE on; CMake probes lscpu and sets AVX512 OFF with only a configure-log warning when a required feature is missing. On a build host without AVX-512 the AMX-off row silently becomes an 8-lane build, and the AMX-on row fails with a #error. The design names sw-dev-01 as the build host without stating that it has AVX-512, and does not name the cross-avx512 preset (BUILD_NATIVE OFF, AVX512 ON) that exists for this case." Recommendation as written, plus: say that the AMX-on row fails loudly, so the log check matters for the AMX-off row.
Three small corrections, none of which changes the verdict. 1. Title wording: "silently overridden" overstates; CMake prints message(WARNING "Disabling AVX512: ...") [CMakeLists.txt@2880c3aa9b:113]. The claim body already says "with only a warning"; use that in the title: "-DAVX512=ON overridden with only a configure warning on a host without AVX-512". 2. Line range: the cross-avx512 preset object spans CMakePresets.json@2880c3aa9b:40-48 (the "name" line is 41), not 41-49. 3. Add to the recommendation, because the design also runs make test-host on the build host (design.txt:430-431): the repository's make path auto-selects preset cross-avx512 when /proc/cpuinfo lacks avx512f and then sets CANNOT_RUN_SLICE_TESTS with the warning "executable will not run on this machine" [GNUmakefile@2880c3aa9b:226-246]. So the design must say where the 16-lane test binaries EXECUTE, not only where they build: on an AVX-512 host, via REMOTE=1 / bin/slice route (doc/slice.md@2880c3aa9b:34-42) or on delphi-3bda under the lease rules the design already gives. Suggested sentence for "Build configurations": "State whether the build host has AVX-512 (grep -c avx512f /proc/cpuinfo). If it does not, configure the two 16-lane rows with preset cross-avx512 (BUILD_NATIVE=OFF, AVX512=ON) and run their tests on an AVX-512 host; in every case confirm the configure log has no 'Disabling AVX512' warning before counting a run as 16-lane."
Omission, low consequence: the table's cmake --preset native -DAVX512=ON rows (design.txt:411-412) leave the BUILD_NATIVE lscpu probe on (CMakeLists.txt:38, 95-117@2880c3aa9b). On a host without the six probed AVX-512 features the probe sets AVX512 OFF with only a warning. In that case the "16-lane, AMX code off" row silently becomes an 8-lane build, while the "16-lane, AMX code on" row fails loudly with #error "TRON_AMX_DISPATCH requires TRON_CHUNK_SIZE == 16" (self_attention.hpp:30-35@2880c3aa9b). The design's named build host sw-dev-01 is recorded as an AMD Ryzen 9 9950X (Zen 5) with AVX-512 (memory note 2026-08-28; not re-measured), and the design's make build-test path auto-selects the cross-avx512 preset on non-AVX-512 hosts (GNUmakefile:226-231@2880c3aa9b), so the scenario does not arise on the documented path. Optional one-sentence addition under Build configurations: confirm the configure log has no "Disabling AVX512" warning; on a host without AVX-512 use --preset cross-avx512 (BUILD_NATIVE OFF, AVX512 ON), whose binaries run only on an AVX-512 host.
Recommendation
Add one sentence under Build configurations: 'Confirm the configure log contains no "Disabling AVX512" warning. On a host without AVX-512, configure with -DBUILD_NATIVE=OFF (preset cross-avx512) for the 16-lane rows; those binaries can only run on an AVX-512 host.'

handoff-actionability; 3 of 3 verifiers could not refute it (votes and corrections)

Minor N41. make iwyu-check called required; make lint absent

revision of F49

touches F49

Note M07. detail::vnni_access: one struct for two view families in two headers, or two structs, is unstated

Where
5. Interface design (Raw storage access row); 7. Then adapt PR #4424 (child step 4 and the t_k_vnni_layout paragraph)
Round-1 link
touches N58, N59, C04
Claim
Ambiguity (child scope). The reviewer's sketch has one detail::vnni_access whose packed_data is a template over vnni_view<T, Layout>. The design keeps the name for the V view and reuses it for the K view, but it splits the types into h/tron/tensor/vnni.hpp (V) and h/tron/tensor/k_vnni.hpp (K). A friend declaration must name one struct, so either k_vnni.hpp includes vnni.hpp to befriend the same struct and vnni_access gains overloads for both families, or each header defines its own access struct under a different name. The design says neither, and N58 and N59 do not cover this.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
Adapt t_k_vnni_layout to create actual aligned k_vnni_tensor owners and call typed store/load/block/reader operations through as_view(). Retain the independent index expectations. For physical-byte checks, copy the trivially copyable owner's object representation into a test buffer. Do not use detail::vnni_access to recreate raw production calls.
Ben comment 5765866077 (github-review.json comments[3]) section 4: namespace detail { struct vnni_access { template <typename T, typename Layout> static T* packed_data(vnni_view<T, Layout> view) noexcept { return view.data_; } }; }. design.txt:223 ("detail::vnni_access is the internal friend"), :385 ("Do not use detail::vnni_access to recreate raw production calls"), :352 (vnni.hpp, new), :369 ("Define k_vnni_tensor, k_vnni_view, and its row expression in h/tron/tensor/k_vnni.hpp").
Correction from verification
The claim stands as written. Refine the recommendation's option list: besides (a) k_vnni.hpp includes vnni.hpp and befriends the same struct, and (b) a separately named detail::k_vnni_access, there is (c) the form in Ben's sketch: k_vnni.hpp forward-declares namespace detail { struct vnni_access; } and befriends it, with one definition of vnni_access (a template over any packed view type) kept in vnni.hpp or a small shared header that h/tron/kernels/k_vnni.hpp includes. The design should name one of the three in child step 4 (design.txt:369) or next to the t_k_vnni_layout sentence (design.txt:385).
One clause of the finding's mechanism sentence is too narrow, not wrong. A C++ friend declaration with a qualified name (friend struct detail::vnni_access;) needs only a prior declaration of that struct, not its definition. Ben's sketch does exactly this with namespace detail { struct vnni_access; } before the view (github-review.json comments[3].body, section 2). So tensor/k_vnni.hpp does not have to include tensor/vnni.hpp to befriend the same struct; a forward declaration suffices. What still must be decided is where the one definition of packed_data lives and how it accepts both view families: a generic template over any befriending view type in a header both families' kernels include, or overloads that require the definition to see k_vnni_view, or a second struct under a different name (for example detail::k_vnni_access). The finding's conclusion that the design states none of this stands; its recommendation's parenthetical "(or a template over both view families)" already covers the generic-template option. Suggested wording for child step 4: "detail::vnni_access is declared once (forward declaration in each view header) and defined once in h/tron/tensor/vnni.hpp as a template over any befriending view type; k_vnni_view befriends it" or "the K family defines its own detail::k_vnni_access", with one clause saying why.
What survives is a wording nit, not an ambiguity requiring a structural sentence: design.txt:385 uses the V family's struct name detail::vnni_access inside the K test paragraph, while :80 says the K family has its own duplicated access plumbing. Change :385 to "Do not use the K family's internal accessor (the K counterpart of detail::vnni_access) to recreate raw production calls." Do not add the recommended "k_vnni.hpp includes vnni.hpp" sentence: a forward declaration of the friend struct suffices, as Ben's sketch shows.
Recommendation
Add one sentence to child step 4: "detail::vnni_access is defined once in h/tron/tensor/vnni.hpp; k_vnni.hpp includes vnni.hpp, k_vnni_view befriends the same struct, and packed_data gains an overload (or a template over both view families)." Or name a separate detail::k_vnni_access and say why.

child-needs, cpp-interface; 2 of 3 verifiers could not refute it (votes and corrections)

Note M08. heterogeneous_scheduler_compile.cpp V fixture is not 64-byte aligned; the aligned row view would inherit that

Where
7. Implementation steps (step 6, source-row fixtures); 5. One cache row-write rule
Round-1 link
touches N15, C03
Claim
Omission, low risk. The row-write rule says sources keep 'the caller's scalar type and alignment' and step 6 says to update source-row fixtures. One fixture has no declared alignment: the set_v call in the heterogeneous scheduler compile test passes std::array<float, 256> values declared without alignas. Today's float set_v body loads it with _mm512_load_ps, a 64-byte-aligned load, so the call already relies on the stack layout. Wrapping that array in an aligned row view does not add a check: the view constructors take the pointer without an alignment assertion, and slice asserts only offsets. If the parent keeps aligned loads (N15's recommendation), the fixture should be declared alignas(64), as every t_llama_unit fixture is; otherwise the latent misalignment stays invisible in the new typed call.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
Packed overloads take v_vnni_view<bf16,R,D> or v_vnni_view<const bf16,R,D>. Sources use the existing contiguous row view with the caller's scalar type and alignment. Preserve those properties at the call site.

Also note the finding's condition "if the parent keeps aligned loads (N15's recommendation)" is already satisfied by the design itself at design.txt:241 ("append_v_row moves today's set_v vector bodies"), so the recommendation does not depend on N15 being accepted.
Facts stand. Refine two points. Claim: the set_v call at heterogeneous_scheduler_compile.cpp:441-444 violates the 64-byte contract of _mm512_load_ps (kv_cache.hpp:2380, :2389); today it passes either by stack layout or by the compiler folding the load into a misalignment-tolerant EVEX memory operand (unverified without the built binary). Note N51. 'Set dma = false' has no referent; reason imprecise

Where
5. Interface design (Owner and expression reads)
Claim
Ambiguity: v_vnni_view<T, Rows, Cols> as declared has no dma (and no aligned) template parameter, so 'Set dma = false' must mean a static member trait like tensor::dma / dmatensor::dma. The only reader of such a trait is launch_matmul_for in tensor.hpp, which consults Activation::dma and Result::dma of matmul OWNER tensors; no designed consumer of the packed view reads it. The stated reason (row values not consecutive) is consistent with views.hpp, which says DMA-safety logically requires contiguity, but the flag's documented meaning is 'refers to DMA-safe memory', and the KV arena IS DMA memory (allocate_kv_group uses try_make_unique_dma_for_overwrite). The sentence therefore neither names a template parameter that exists nor a consumer that cares.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
The row stores its pair address and even/odd position by value. It remains valid after a temporary view object disappears, while the owner remains alive. Set dma = false: logical row values are not consecutive in memory.
design.txt:199-201 (declarations: template <typename T, size_t Rows, size_t Cols> class v_vnni_view;) and :212; h/tron/tensor/views.hpp:11-21@2880c3aa9b (dma = 'DMA-safe memory ... Logically, being DMA-safe requires contiguity ... but we track that separately'); h/tron/tensor/tensor.hpp:35@2880c3aa9b (static constexpr bool dma = false;) and :411-416 (launch_matmul_for = t_launch_matmul<..., Activation::dma, Result::dma>); h/tron/hardware/dmatensor.hpp:38@2880c3aa9b (static constexpr bool dma = true;); h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp:1199-1208@2880c3aa9b (allocate_kv_group: hardware::try_make_unique_dma_for_overwrite).
Correction from verification
Two additions, no change of substance. (1) The static-member precedent has three files, not two: h/tron/tensor/tensor.hpp:35,254 and h/tron/tensor/itensor.hpp:26 (static constexpr bool dma = false;) beside h/tron/hardware/dmatensor.hpp:38,173 (= true). (2) The revised sentence is a regression from the original: round-1 design-text.txt:246 read "Set dma = false to mark the logical row as unsuitable for direct row DMA" (close to views.hpp:11-15), and round-1 accepted it; the revision replaced it with the contiguity reason without being asked. Suggested text for design.txt:212: "The packed view and row have no aligned or dma template parameters. Following tensor.hpp:35 they may expose static constexpr bool dma = false: the flag means the object can be a DMA transfer source [views.hpp:11-15], and a pair-interleaved row cannot be one although the arena itself is DMA memory [kv_cache.hpp:1199-1208]. No consumer in this design reads it; the only reader in the repository is launch_matmul_for [tensor.hpp:411-416]."
Keep the finding at note level with the "replace" option, and drop the "delete" option (deleting would remove text that round 1 relied on). Replacement text for design.txt:212: "Expose static constexpr bool dma = false, following the convention for expr-derived types that are not views [h/tron/tensor/tensor.hpp:35, :254; itensor.hpp:26 @2880c3aa9b]. The arena is DMA memory, but a pair-interleaved row is not a contiguous DMA transfer source [h/tron/tensor/views.hpp:11-15]; the existing k_view over the same arena also passes dma = false [h/tron/models/kv_cache.hpp:2210, :2217]. No consumer in this design reads the trait; the only reader in the tree is launch_matmul_for [tensor.hpp:411-416]." Also soften the finding's claim "has no referent" to "does not say the referent is a static member; the codebase convention makes it determinable", and note that the revision removed the two clauses ("unsuitable for direct row DMA"; "Allocation in DMA memory does not make the row's values consecutive") that round 1 accepted at the original's line 246.
Recommendation
Either delete the sentence or replace it with: 'The packed view and row carry no aligned/dma template parameters. For consistency with tensor.hpp:35 they may expose static constexpr bool dma = false; no consumer in this design reads it. The arena is DMA memory, but a pair-interleaved row is not a contiguous DMA transfer source (views.hpp:13-15).'

Claim

v_vnni_view<T,R,D> inherits expr<view_type,R,D>. v_vnni_row<T,D> inherits expr<row_type,D>. Restrict T to bf16 or const bf16.
