Note N53. Slice-scope sentence misreadable as 'no slice path exists'

Where
revision of F08
Round-1 link
touches F08
Claim
Kind: ambiguity (c). Claim: the sentence at design.txt:277 "This scope decision removes the unneeded odd-boundary slice-write and temporary-slice lifetime questions" can be read as "no slice path exists for a packed view", while the generic expr slice overload still binds v_vnni_view and captures it by reference. Evidence: slice.hpp:147-177 @f46e48bab498 (unchanged at 2880c3aa9b); expr.hpp:695-732 @2880c3aa9b (slice_expr needs only chunk/bf16_chunk/fp16_chunk of the row, which design.txt:220-221 provide); design.txt:211 (view inherits expr<view_type,R,D>). Recommendation: the one-sentence residual above; no code change needed while no caller slices a packed plane.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
design.txt:63 "Limit the parent: expression reads and existing row operations. Defer slices, iteration, generic expression assignment, and expression constructors until a caller needs them." design.txt:85 "Is the smaller parent scope sufficient? | Yes. Include expression reads and typed bulk operations; defer the general tensor write and slice interfaces." design.txt:275-277 "Explicitly deferred. No packed slices or iterators; no generic row.set, set_pair, matrix.set, or owner expression constructor/assignment; no runtime shapes or general transpose interface. Leave h/tron/tensor/slice.hpp unchanged. Existing ordinary tensors may still be constructed from a packed view's read expressions. This scope decision removes the unneeded odd-boundary slice-write and temporary-slice lifetime questions." design.txt:352 step 1 "... No change to slice.hpp." design.txt:211 "v_vnni_view<T,R,D> inherits expr<view_type,R,D>. v_vnni_row<T,D> inherits expr<row_type,D>." design.txt:466 disposition "F08 F34 F37 | Scope reduced explicitly | Defer slices, iteration, generic assignment, and owner expression construction. The owner is not an expression. No shared slice-header change." The verification table at design.txt:393-401 has no row about slice lifetime.
Correction from verification
Kind: omission (d) with a minor over-claim in one sentence. The revised design leaves slice.hpp unchanged and adds no packed slice type, which correctly closes the speculative-code part of F08, but it omits the sentence the round-1 F08 verifier corrections asked for: that the existing generic expression slice [h/tron/tensor/slice.hpp:147-177, unchanged at 2880c3aa9b] already accepts a packed view through its rank-2 expr base [design.txt:211] and captures it by reference [slice.hpp:168-169], and whether the parent leaves that path as is or adds one deleted overload. The last sentence of design.txt:277 ("removes ... temporary-slice lifetime questions") is true only for the design's own dropped slice type. Corrected recommendation text for design.txt:277: "The existing generic expression slice [slice.hpp:147-177] already accepts a packed view through its rank-2 expr base and captures it by reference. Slice only a named lvalue view; do not pass a temporary view, and do not pass the owner itself to slice<>(): its Owner& overload [slice.hpp:38-42] calls as_view() on an lvalue, so the deleted rvalue forms do not apply, and the temporary view it produces is captured by reference and destroyed before the result is used. This design leaves that path as is and adds no deleted overload." The cited expression header is h/tron/kernels/expr.hpp (lines 45-66, 119-126, 140-147, 693-732 @2880c3aa9b), not h/tron/tensor/. Severity minor rather than note: one sentence must change, low risk, no caller today at main or in PR 4424 slices a cache plane (r1-findings.md F08 evidence).
The finding's recommendation wording "do not pass a temporary view, or a temporary owner through the Owner& overload at slice.hpp:38-42" is imprecise in two ways. (1) A non-const temporary owner cannot bind Owner& at all (slice.hpp:40 takes Owner& owner; only a const-qualified rvalue owner binds, as the round-1 F34 verifier corrections state). (2) The Owner& route is hazardous for ANY owner, including an ordinary lvalue owner. slice.hpp:40-41 forwards owner.as_view(), a prvalue v_vnni_view. That prvalue is not a view<> or const_view<> specialization and does not derive from const_views<> (design.txt:211 "inherits expr<view_type,R,D>"; :214 "Do not inherit views<>"; :219 no pointer conversion), so the by-value view/const_view overloads (slice.hpp:44-116) and the const_views overload (:118-145) do not deduce. Only the expr<B,in_d0,in_d1> const& overload (:147-177) is viable. Its lambda captures &v_in (:169), and the temporary view bound to v_in is destroyed at the end of the return full-expression at slice.hpp:41. The returned lambda_expr then refers to a dead view. Existing tensor owners do not have this route: their as_view() returns a view<>, which the by-value overload copies with no reference capture. Corrected residual sentence for design.txt:277: "The existing generic expression slice [h/tron/tensor/slice.hpp:147-177] already accepts a packed view through its rank-2 expr base, with the existing offset % 64 and width % 64 rules, and captures the view by reference. Slice only a named lvalue v_vnni_view. Do not pass a temporary view, and do not pass an owner to slice<> at all: the Owner& overload at slice.hpp:38-42 forwards a temporary as_view() result into that reference capture. This design leaves the path as is and adds no deleted overload." Then narrow the last sentence of :277 to "This scope decision removes the odd-boundary slice-write question and the lifetime rules of the design's own slice type; tron's existing expression-slice lifetime rule still applies."
Finding stands as written. Two small tightenings: (1) the design's row need not provide fp16_chunk itself, because the rank-1 expr<B,N> base supplies it from chunk() [h/tron/kernels/expr.hpp:113-120 @2880c3aa9b], so "which design.txt:220-221 provide" should read "chunk and bf16_chunk from design.txt:220-221, fp16_chunk from the expr base"; (2) the generic path only compiles for column extents and offsets that are multiples of 64 [slice.hpp:159-162 @f46e48bab498], which the residual sentence could mention.
Recommendation
Add one sentence to "Explicitly deferred" (design.txt:277): "The existing generic expression slice [h/tron/tensor/slice.hpp:147-177] already accepts an lvalue packed view through its rank-2 expr base and captures it by reference; do not pass a temporary view, or a temporary owner through the Owner& overload at slice.hpp:38-42, to slice<>. This design leaves that path as is and adds no deleted overload." Then narrow the last sentence of 277 to "This scope decision removes the odd-boundary slice-write question and the lifetime questions of the design's own slice type."
Found by
dispo:B05; 3 of 3 verifiers could not refute it (votes and corrections)

Note N60. cmake preset flags vs make targets: cache caveats unstated

Where
8. Verification and acceptance (Build configurations; Commands and execution hosts)
Round-1 link
touches F47
Claim
Ambiguity: the design gives three cmake --preset native -D... configurations and then says to run make build-test and make test-host. Those Make targets reconfigure gen/ with Make's own preset choice and flag list, which never mentions AVX512 or TRON_AMX_DISPATCH; the hand-passed values survive only as CMake cache entries in gen/. On an AVX-512 host (sw-dev-01 is one: Ryzen 9 9950X passes the probe's six features) this works, but on a host without AVX-512 Make selects cross-avx512, whose preset forces AVX512=ON over a hand -DAVX512=OFF, and refuses to run tests. The native preset also pins binaryDir to gen/, so the three configurations cannot be three build directories in one checkout; each needs a full reconfigure or its own worktree. The Nix lane uses BUILD_NATIVE=OFF, so cross-avx512 is the closer local equivalent of the required lane than native.
Evidence
Design quote and evidence
Use separate build directories at the recorded baseline and parent head. Use identical compilers and flags for each pair. Run the relevant existing targets through the repository's documented build commands.
design.txt lines 407, 411-413, 430-431. GNUmakefile@2880c3aa9b:227-231 (HAS_AVX512 from /proc/cpuinfo selects native or cross-avx512), :538-543 (gen/config/test: rm -rf gen/config then cmake --preset $(CMAKE_PRESET) -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=... -DBUILD_TEST_MODELS=ON ... with no AVX512/TRON_AMX_DISPATCH), :883-885 and :904-906 (test targets exit 1 when cross-compiled). CMakePresets.json@2880c3aa9b:31-39 native binaryDir ${sourceDir}/gen; :41-47 cross-avx512 cacheVariables BUILD_NATIVE OFF, AVX512 ON. CMakeLists.txt@2880c3aa9b:95-116 probe features avx512f dq bw vl vbmi2 vpopcntdq. nix/cmake-tron-test-build.nix@2880c3aa9b:86-88 -DBUILD_NATIVE=OFF -DAVX512=ON. README.ci.md@2880c3aa9b:578-580. Memory claude-box-tron-build-env.md:11 (sw-dev-01 = AMD Ryzen 9 9950X, no AMX; TRON_AMX_DISPATCH=ON configured there).
Correction from verification
Keep the finding, but replace the sentence "The native preset also pins binaryDir to gen/, so the three configurations cannot be three build directories in one checkout; each needs a full reconfigure or its own worktree" with: "The native preset sets binaryDir to gen/ [CMakePresets.json@2880c3aa9b:35]. A command-line -B <dir> overrides that [CMake 3.22 manual cmake.1.rst:391-393], so three build directories in one checkout are possible. The documented Make targets cannot use them: make build-test, make build-test-host and make test-host hardcode gen [GNUmakefile@2880c3aa9b:351-353, 360-368, 905-906]. So either switch configurations in gen/ by a full reconfigure, use one worktree per configuration, or build and run the extra configurations with cmake --build <dir> and bin/slice run --build-dir=<dir> outside the Make targets." Also state that the test refusal on a non-AVX-512 host applies only when REMOTE is not enabled [GNUmakefile:240-247], and correct the evidence range ":904-906" to ":901-903". Expect that baseline result; it is not the parent's fault." Then resolve the "may" at line 254: either (a) the parent adds the unified typed set_v/get_v wrapper to the 8-lane page (forwarding to v().set()), in which case say that these two tests first compile in the 8-lane configuration at parent head and that the 8-lane row's baseline comparison covers only the library and the unconditional fake AMX definitions; or (b) the parent does not, in which case the 8-lane row must exclude t_llama_unit and t_heterogeneous_scheduler from what it builds and the definition-of-done line 452 must say so.
The claim is right for t_heterogeneous_scheduler but too narrow: t_llama_unit, the design's primary behavior-test target (design.txt:398, 401), also cannot compile in the 8-lane build at unmodified main, so the fix belongs in the 8-lane row at design.txt:413, not only in the line-400 row. Evidence at t/t_llama_unit.cpp@2880c3aa9b (unchanged since 98bb8cb22f94 per facts-r2): non-template TEST_CASE "book.append()" [:2482] calls dst_page.set_v / expected_page.set_v on a concrete book<2,4,128,0>::page_t at :2533, :2535, :2568, :2569, :2690, :2705, :2721 and get_v at :2539; TEST_CASE "scaled_v padding NaN" [:2658] calls p->set_v at :2792 and :2802; TEMPLATE_TEST_CASE "scaled_v" [:1665] calls p->set_v at :1723 for two unconditionally listed cache types; template check_wide_scaled_v [:125-142, set_v at :142] is instantiated for 256 and 512 by TEST_CASE "scaled_v supports wide values" [:1199-1207]. None of these sit inside the file's TRON_CHUNK_SIZE guards (the guarded blocks are 793-800, 817-823, 1551-1555, 1573-1578, 1732-1767, 1922-1926). Corrected recommendation: in the 8-lane row (design.txt:413) add "At unmodified main, t_llama_unit and t_heterogeneous_scheduler do not compile in this configuration because page::set_v exists only under TRON_CHUNK_SIZE == 16 (kv_cache.hpp:1616-1674); make build-test therefore fails at baseline. The 8-lane check covers the tron library and the unconditional AMX test substitutes (section 5, design.txt:270); record the baseline test-build failure rather than fixing those tests in the parent." Not compiled; inferred from declarations. Insufficient data on whether the tron library target itself compiles with AVX512=OFF at main; a baseline cmake --preset native -DAVX512=OFF build of target tron would resolve it.

5. The four questions for the reviewer

Round 1 asked codex to list its departures from Ben's sketch as questions for him instead of asserting them. The revision has a four-row "Questions for the reviewer" table with a proposed answer per row, marked as proposals pending agreement. The table below checks whether the four questions cover every departure and whether an implementer can act on them.

Design question and proposed answerCoversAssessment
Q1. May the address formula live in each concrete view? Proposed: yes, with separate V and K families and separate owners.Folding the layout type into the view (Ben's reservation, jhan's reading) and the two-family consequence (no single vnni_tensor<Layout> template).Right question and right proposed answer. The cost of two families (duplicated conversion and access plumbing) is stated in the answer, which is enough for Ben to weigh it.
Q2. Should a const view object prevent mutation of its elements? Proposed: yes, at both matrix and row rank.The departure from Ben's std::span rule (round-1 F04).Right question. The revision now says the repository precedent is mixed (design section 2) and chooses the matrix rule for both new types. It cites only the rank-2 source; the rank-1 comment that states Ben's rule is not linked (N46, note).
Q3. May the view provide operator[] as well as at(token, dimension)? Proposed: yes; at() stays as an equivalent bounds-checked spelling.The departure from Ben's at()-only access (round-1 F02).Right question. It should say why the expr base needs operator[] (it indexes rows through it) and why Ben's sketch had no operator[] (a two-argument accessor cannot be spelled with one subscript), so Ben sees the trade (N09, minor).
Q4. Is the smaller parent scope sufficient? Proposed: yes; slices, iteration and generic writes are deferred.The scope cut round 1 asked for (F08) versus Ben's "usual tensor operations".Right question. The deferred list in section 5 is concrete. The design should say which sections change if Ben answers no (N07, note).
Missing: who asked for native K in the parent, and does Ben agree?Typing the native-K kernel parameter goes beyond Ben's sketch, which types only the packed planes. jhan asked for it (prompt item 4, issue #4525).Design section 2 calls it "the requested parent scope" without naming the requester, in a table whose other rows are Ben's requests. Name jhan as the requester and either add a fifth question or say that jhan owns this decision (N08, minor). All three judges rated the questions block inadequate on this point.

One gap applies to every row: the design never says where an answer is recorded or what the implementer does if no answer exists when work starts, while the definition of done requires recorded answers (N04, major). The recommended fix names the record (a comment on issue #4525 posted by jhan, copied into the parent PR description under a Decisions heading) and the fallback (implement the proposed answers and mark each decision as proposed).

6. What the revision gets right

  • Section 6 arena construction closes F12 and F40 well: the array placement-new form is the right one, std::construct_at/T()/T{} are excluded with the correct reason, std::start_lifetime_as is removed for the pinned toolchain, the helper runs in allocate_kv_group after install and before publish, the zero-byte early return is kept, and layout_t::storage_location plus slot_base are described as the code does them (kv_cache.hpp:727-736, 1188-1198 at 2880c3aa9b).
  • Section 5's even_part/odd_part description and the two scaled_v bounds ((count + 1) / 2 * 2 for 16 lanes, count for 8 lanes) match kv_cache.hpp:2000-2007, 2078 and 2139-2175 exactly (F42 closed).
  • Section 6's NaN paragraph corrects the round-1 page's own error: fp32s_to_bf16s sets the lowest retained mantissa bit, not the quiet bit (bf16.hpp:92-109), so the revision is more accurate than the review it answered (F41 partly).
  • Section 5 correctly identifies seq and sseq as alias templates that cannot be forward-declared and keeps amx_attn_iface.hpp intrinsics-free (F43 corrected against seq.hpp:21-25).
  • Section 2 turns the round-1 'deviates from Ben without asking' finding into a question table with proposed answers and states plainly that they are proposals, not recorded approval (F02).
  • Section 7's child section now states the K store lane rule correctly in substance (a K row write never reads, merges and rewrites a whole 64-byte line, because the shared block save writes disjoint lanes of one line from several threads), and set_k_block keeps 16 independent source rows with null for absent lanes (C04, C05).
  • Section 7 gives the stacked-child mechanics (gh pr edit --base, keep draft, preserve the old head locally, reconstruct as topical commits rather than replaying 22 commits) that F14 asked for.
  • Section 8 refuses to invent numbers ('Insufficient data: there is no parent implementation or runtime measurement here'), states the lease semantics as lib-guard.sh implements them, and points the required-CI AMX gap at the existing issue #3997 instead of asking the implementer to edit CI (F16, C08).
  • Section 3 fixes the inverted main-tip label and the 'before #4424' wording (F05, F50) and lists the five step-list files that moved after f46e48ba.
  • Section 9 names every one of the 58 retained ids and the 10 rejected ids correctly, and does not reinstate any rejected candidate as a defect.

7. Recommended edits, in the order to make them

  1. Section 2 and the definition of done (N04, N08). Add: "Answers are recorded as a comment on issue #4525, posted by jhan, and copied into the parent PR description under a Decisions heading. If no answer exists when implementation starts, implement the Proposed answer column and mark each decision as proposed." Change the definition-of-done item to "Each reviewer question has an answer recorded in the parent PR description, marked accepted or proposed, and the implemented interface matches it." At the native-K bullet, name jhan (issue #4525) as the requester and either add a fifth question for Ben or state that jhan owns the decision.
  2. 8-lane build guidance (N01, M01, N61, N16, N12, N23, N24). Replace the both-chunk-width sentence with: "The class definitions of v_vnni_tensor, v_vnni_view and v_vnni_row are complete in both builds (members are a bf16 array, a pointer and small integers). Their operation bodies (append_v_row, load_row, copy_token, chunk, bf16_chunk) and every packed-V test case compile only under TRON_CHUNK_SIZE == 16. The typed kernel declarations and the fake definitions stay unconditional." Rewrite the 8-lane row: at unmodified main, t_llama_unit and t_heterogeneous_scheduler do not compile in this configuration, because page::set_v and page::get_v exist only under TRON_CHUNK_SIZE == 16 [kv_cache.hpp:1616-1674]; the 8-lane check for the parent is configure, build the tron library and t_amx_dispatch_dtype, run t_amx_dispatch_dtype. Then decide the 8-lane set_v story once: either the parent adds an 8-lane page::set_v forwarder and removes the #if in save_v_impl, or it keeps v(...).set() and the #if; give the per-build width rules (16-lane set_v/get_v head_size % 32, scaled_v_expr {64,128,256,512}; 8-lane scaled_v_expr head_size % 64) and drop the 8-lane branch of page::v_tensor unless a caller is named.
  3. Child table (N03). Add a row: "detail::kv_block K member: when k_vnni::layout_on<head_size> holds, the K member is k_vnni_tensor<page_size, head_size>, selected with std::conditional_t against the existing bf16s array as Ben's sketch shows. Keep sizeof(k) == sizeof(v), 64-byte alignment and trivial default construction so the parent's block-array construction covers it. The packed-plane accessor (name it) returns kv_block<geometry>(slot, kv_head).k.as_view(), never a cast; the four reinterpret_casts of the array at the child head [kv_cache.hpp@30c4ac82cb:1692, 1706, 1730, 1742] go away with it."
  4. NaN paragraph and the Bits and conversions test row (N02, F41). Add: "TRON_IGNORE_NAN is defined for every non-Debug configuration [src/tron/CMakeLists.txt:292-295, a PUBLIC option of tron]. The native and deb presets and the required Nix test build are RelWithDebInfo, so in every configuration in section 8 both paths truncate identically (0x7F800001 becomes 0x7F80 on both). The 0x7F81 result appears only in the Debug presets asan, tsan and coverage." In the test row: guard generic-path NaN expectations with #ifdef TRON_IGNORE_NAN, or check the generic path only with finite, denormal and infinity inputs. Give the concrete bit example round 1 asked for (0x7F800001 -> 0x7F80 versus 0x7F81) and drop the fp16 case, since _mm512_cvtph_ps quiets the NaN and neither path yields infinity for it.
  5. Section 9 (M05, N44, N43). Add a column "Verifier corrections applied" (yes / partly / no) or split the 20 partly-addressed ids into rows labelled "Partly; remaining: ...". Replace "including its nine later corrections" with a sentence that says the nine C.. ids are the round-1 critic's additions. Say once what a bracketed id such as [F05] means and where the round-1 page is. Apply the F25 vocabulary fix the table already marks as accepted ("VNNI K layout", "shared block save", save = operation and store = instruction).
  6. Round-1 residuals that are one-sentence fixes. F15/N29: "ten of the same files", and tell the agent to re-measure the conflict set with git merge-tree against the live tip (N30). F48/N37: the guard refuses when the CI lease is busy, when another person is active on the machine, or when a blackout window is set, and campaign_guard_acquire refuses while a runtron is running. F49/N41: make lint is the required check; make iwyu-check is an optional extra because the parent edits no public API header; name the platform key of the benchmark records (N38). F35/N13: omitting bf16_chunk silently selects the fp32 fallback, which is bit-exact but slower; a wrong signature is a compile error, not a silent fallback; detect the omission with a static_assert on the member type, not with a bit-pattern test. C02/N27: token_ix_needed is the page-readiness rule, not a partner rule. C06/N28: name the two Notes by their real titles and lines. C01/F12/N25: EAGLE exists only with uniform geometry, so the whole-arena array already covers its slot and the heterogeneous pass never meets it. F30/N45: say in the Short version that native K gets aliases and typed kernel parameters, not a new storage type. F38: add the V row unpack in full.hpp to the inspected call sites. F54: give the tile formula with its two block indices. F57: link the kernel signatures at f46e48ba. F58: state the rule files are byte-identical by blob id at the five commits. F08/N53: say the existing generic slice still accepts an lvalue packed view; only new slice code is deferred. C07/N57: say where k_vnni::index lives after the move. F29/N33: name save_k and save_k_helper and the two child test files the worker-index change touches.
  7. Callers and mechanisms the tables miss (M02, M03, M04, N15, N17). Add book::fill_storage_slot / fill_random (test-only whole-block byte fill) to the callers table and to the search pattern; say it stays as is. Name the compile-check mechanism: requires-expression constants with STATIC_REQUIRE_FALSE next to the existing ones in t_llama_unit.cpp:84-123, and rewrite page_supports_uniform_kv_access for the typed get_v destination. Name book::for_each_active_slot as the compile-time geometry loop for the heterogeneous construction pass, selecting slots by storage_location(...).reclaimable. State the aligned flag of the typed set_v source view at the model call site. Remove copy_plane or name its caller; the owner's defaulted copy already gives a byte copy.
  8. Interface details (N14, N20, N22, N51). Say which header defines the native_k_view aliases and that the Stride default lives in view.hpp, so the interface header forward-declares the primary template without repeating the default. Extend the no-public-pointer rule to v_vnni_row's constructor. Admit that the K alias equals today's view type, so it rejects raw pointers but accepts any 64x128 bf16 view. Give dma its documented meaning (DMA-safe memory, contiguity tracked separately) or drop the reason clause.
  9. CI evidence (N36, N39). Say what counts as execution evidence: the GCP Nix Test host job log of a run whose test steps ran (first attempt with a changed test runtime, or a GitHub rerun), showing Slice executing the named cases; a green check whose steps were skipped by a cached passage marker is not evidence. Note that the native preset probes the host and turns AVX512 off on a host without it, so -DAVX512=ON is not a guarantee.
  10. Words and page (N05, N06, N11, N48, N62). Define "the reviewer" once (Ben, the tron maintainer) and separate the two Claude sessions by role. Define pair_base, kv_block_t, region_base, block_count and tile at first use. Say whose interpretation "The interpretation" is (jhan's). Name the live main tip at review time (2880c3aa9b) next to the recorded tip. Replace the 59 non-ASCII characters (title em dash, curly quotes, arrows, multiplication signs) with ASCII or numeric entities if the page is ever published as an artifact.
  11. Issue body (F19, F20, F21, N42, M06). Keep the GitHub edits out of the design, but add a block "Proposed issue #4525 body additions (owner: jhan)" with the problem sentence, the three layout definitions, Ben's three straw-man constraints, and the roles-not-names wording, so jhan can paste it. Say how the parent PR body references the issue ("Refs #4525", not a closing keyword, because the child step follows).

8. Verdict

Three judges read the revised design with the dispositions and the confirmed new findings in hand, each from a different position: the implementing agent, the maintainer who wrote the straw-man, and a risk reviewer. All three returned the same verdict, accept with changes, and all three said the text is not yet implementable without a question to jhan or Ben beyond the four listed ones. All three also judged the questions block inadequate because native-K typing is not put to Ben.

I agree with the judges. The 16-lane production path, which is the path that ships, is now specified correctly against live main: the save, load and copy chains, the partner-zero rule, the scaled_v bounds, the arena construction form and the lease rules all match the source. Nothing in the residual list would silently corrupt an attention sum. The four majors are about the 8-lane configuration, a test expectation, the child's K storage, and process; each is a one-paragraph edit.

Must change before implementation starts: edits 1 to 5 in section 7 (N04 and N08; N01 with M01 and N61; N03; N02; M05). Everything else can be fixed while implementing, and the implementer should treat the partly rows of section 3 as a checklist.

10. Method, evidence, and limits

This round ran as one workflow of 268 agents (about 29.7 million tokens, 4,716 tool calls, 1 hour 51 minutes). Every agent read the same verified-facts files (round-1 facts and a round-2 addendum I verified myself against live main 2880c3aa9b before the run) and had read-only access to the tron worktree at the pinned commits, Ben's comments, jhan's prompts, the round-1 findings and the revised design text.

Disposition phase: 15 batches of related round-1 findings (2 to 4 ids each). One checker per batch graded each id and reported any new defect the fix introduced; two adversarial re-graders (source-truth, fairness) re-graded every id; the majority decided. 45 agents. Result: 35 resolved, 20 partly, 3 deferred, no split votes.

New-defect phase: 8 finders (C++ interface, behavior and arena construction, process and CI, handoff actionability, fidelity to Ben, simplicity and scope, formulas and page quality, the child's needs) produced 81 candidates; the checkers added 17 more; a merge agent grouped the 98 into 62. Three verifiers per candidate tried to refute each one; 56 survived and 6 were rejected (section 9). A completeness critic added 8 candidates, and all 8 survived the same verification, for 64 new findings. Three judges then gave independent verdicts.

Limits. Nothing was compiled or run; the claim that the two test programs do not compile in the 8-lane build (M01, N61) is inferred from the #if guards in the source, not from a build. The design's pinned links were re-validated structurally (every local target exists; the 38 GitHub blob links are the same set as in round 1) but not fetched. This page was checked for pure ASCII, for an anchor per round-1 id, and for resolving local links; no browser render check was run on this host.

  • Data and scripts: evidence/claude-review-r2/ holds workflow-result.json (every agent's return value), findings.json (the data behind this page), build_findings.py and gen_review.py (the generator), overrides.json (my grades and prose), lead-verified-facts-r2.md (the facts I verified before the run), r1-findings-compact.md (the round-1 findings as the agents read them), design-revised-text.txt and design-revised-sha256.txt (the exact text reviewed).
  • Votes: every new finding's three votes and corrections are on the round-2 votes page; the disposition votes are inside the rows of section 3 (expand "Votes and evidence").
  • Round 1: the original review is archived as claude-review-design-new-tensor-type-r1.html with its data in evidence/claude-review/. The revised design cites its ids; the anchors on this page keep those links working.
  • State at review time (2026-09-22 04:00 to 06:15 UTC): main 2880c3aa9b; PR 4424 draft on main at 30c4ac82cb, no reply after Ben's sketch; issue #4525 open, assigned to jhan, 0 comments, body unchanged since 00:11 UTC; issue #3997 open.
  • Severity re-grades by the lead after reading the votes: N12 to note (two of three verifiers: an omission of the 8-lane rule, not a wrong statement); N07 and N42 to note (the majority of their votes). The four majors kept their grade: N01 (two verifiers said minor on its own, but with M01 and N61 the 8-lane guidance as a whole would send the agent into a baseline compile failure), N02, N03, N04.
